# AMLP - World-Class LPC Driver Roadmap

Goal: transform AMLP from a single-mudlib FluffOS-targeting driver into
the best LPC runtime available - a FluffOS/LDMud-level driver, done
better than either, on their own terms and surpassing both on the
dimensions neither addressed. Scope clarified 2026-08-18: DGD is a
comparison-only reference dialect, not a required target for parity or
completion: real, done-better FluffOS/LDMud compatibility is the actual
goal, not three-way parity across all three. See Phase 1's own header
for what this changes there.

Each source directory carries an `instruct.md` that owns the detailed task
list for that subsystem. This file is the master sequencing reference.

---

## Phase 0 - Stabilize the current base

**Prerequisite for everything else. Do not start Phase 1 until Phase 0 is
complete and the full test suite is passing with no regressions.**

Otherwise complete as of 2026-08-19, see the update immediately below. **greenlit 2026-08-18 as a real multi-session project; first real slice landed that session**, `parse_init()`/`parse_add_rule()`/`parse_dump()`/`parse_remove()` implemented that session, all four backed by a real, faithfully-ported rule-string tokenizer and a real verb/rule registry. **Update, 2026-08-19 (a later session, continuing further):** all 8 named functions are now implemented, `parse_refresh()`/`parse_sentence()`/`parse_add_synonym()` landed in intervening sessions (see this row's own STATUS.md entries for each), and `parse_my_rules()` (both its call-flag and default "return the verb_rule args array" forms, its own live `parse_restricted` handler-scoped matching, and its own still-live "illegal to call parse_sentence() recursively" reentrancy guard, distinct from parse_sentence()'s own matching guard which real source leaves commented out) landed this session, see ParserPackage::parseMyRules()'s own header comment and this row's own STATUS.md entry for the full derivation. One earlier note in this cell's own history was wrong and is corrected here rather than left standing: there is no real "`parse_add_rule`'s 3-arg shadow form", confirmed directly against the real vendored signature list, `packages/parser_spec.c:6`, `void parse_add_rule(string, string);`, exactly two arguments, no variant; whatever that note was pointing at, it was not a real gap. **Update, 2026-08-19 (a further session, evidence-based re-rank): real sentence matching for a two-object rule where BOTH object tokens are singular (OBJ/LIV only, `"give OBJ to LIV"`, `"put OBJ in OBJ"`) is now implemented**, `ParserPackage::dependentCheckFunctions()`/`checkOneRelation()`/`checkObjectRelations()` (real `dependent_check_functions()`/`check_one_relation()`/`check_object_relations()`, packages/parser.c:2184-2493, read and ported in full this session, not just the portion an earlier session's own scoping pass had stopped at), plus a corresponding generalization of `ParserPackage::makeFunction()` (real `make_function()`'s own `omatch`/`which>=4` relational-naming logic, previously hardcoded to the single-object-only case). Picked over the other three real open candidates (LDMud mapping N-width value semantics, row 1.9; LDMud closure kinds, row 1.7/1.8; the narrowed FluffOS `net_dead()` vs. LDMud `disconnect()` question, row 1.4/1.16) on real, corpus-measured evidence, not the "obvious architectural next step" assumption alone: a full re-count across every FluffOS-family corpus vendored in `temp/` found 77 real two-object `parse_add_rule()`/`SetRules()` rules in dead-souls.net's own Dead Souls `lib/verbs/` alone (`give`, `put`, `get`, `pour`, `install`, and more), 59 of which (77%) are both-singular, versus zero real `m_allocate`/`m_entry`/`m_reallocate`/`m_add`/`m_contains`/mapping-width-literal usage in `temp/core-lib` (the one confirmed LDMud-targeting corpus), zero real `lambda()`/`bind_lambda()` usage there (`unbound_lambda()` has exactly 4, all in one master-hook-configuration file, `secure/master/hooks.c`), and zero real per-object `void disconnect(object, string)` master-apply usage anywhere (every "disconnect" hit found was an unrelated same-named database-handle-close local function, `secure/dataServices/dataService.c`). A first attempt at this same investigation undercounted the two-object evidence to zero via a broken regex (a `grep -E` combined-`\b...\b` pattern that silently never matches, confirmed and corrected before trusting the count), re-derived with a direct Python scan of every real `SetRules()`/`parse_add_rule()` call instead, not trusted on the first, wrong number. **A genuinely plural two-object rule (`"give OBS to LIV"`, 18 of the 77 real rules found) originally stayed deferred here via a `VerbRuleNode::hasPluralObjectToken` gate in `ParserPackage::parseRulesFor()`, the same honest "not yet supported, not silently wrong" stance used elsewhere in this row, since removed, see the update immediately below.**

**Update, 2026-08-19 (a further session, same day): the plural side is now real too, and it needed no new production logic at all beyond removing that gate.** A second, fresh full re-read of `check_object_relations()`/`check_one_relation()`/`dependent_check_functions()`, specifically hunting for a separate plural-only code path, found none exists in real source: `direct_unique`/`indirect_unique` (derived straight from each match's own real `PLURAL_MODIFIER` bit) are the *only* plurality-dependent decisions anywhere in `check_object_relations()`, whether an ambiguity check applies at all (a genuinely plural side is allowed to accumulate more than one accepted candidate without erroring, matching real "give all the coins to the guard"), and whether the final resolved value is a single object index or the whole accumulated candidate set, both already ported faithfully in the session that built the both-singular case, since that session ported the whole real function rather than hand-simplifying it. `dependent_check_functions()`/`check_one_relation()` have no plurality-dependent logic at all. Confirmed empirically, not just by re-reading: removing `parseRulesFor()`'s own gate and writing real regression tests for both real dead-souls.net Dead Souls `give.c` shapes (`"OBS LIV"`, plural direct, singular indirect; `"LIV OBS"`, singular direct, plural indirect) exposed zero new production bugs. `VerbRuleNode::hasPluralObjectToken` and its own gate are removed entirely (no longer serve any purpose). One genuine test-authoring pitfall hit and corrected before trusting the result: real `parse_obj()`'s own singular-noun match path strips `PLURAL_MODIFIER` off the resulting match whenever the player types the *singular* noun form, even for an OBS-token rule slot (OBS means "this slot accepts either one object or several," not "always plural", which one actually happens is decided by what the player typed, not by the rule's own declared token). An initial test attempt using the singular noun ("coin") produced a real, correctly-derived `ERR_AMBIG` instead of the intended plural success path, not a driver bug, a test-sentence mistake, fixed by using the real plural noun ("coins", `parse_command_plural_id_list()`) and re-deriving the exact real per-`which` naming by hand (confirmed: only the `do_` call, `which==3`, and a probe of an *earlier* object-family token's own contribution to the name at a *later* `which` value, ever use the "obs"/"lvs" spelling, `can_`/`direct_`/`indirect_` probing a token's *own* narrowing pass always use the short "obj"/"liv" spelling regardless of that token's real plurality, confirmed independently against real dead-souls.net Dead Souls `give.c`'s own function names, which never define a single `can_`/`direct_`/`indirect_` variant with an "obs"/"lvs" segment anywhere, only its three real `do_` variants do). 3 new regression tests (one replacing an old one that, like the both-singular slice's own precedent, had gone accidentally-still-green for an unrelated reason once the gate was removed, its own fixture never defined any `direct_`/`indirect_` function at all, so it kept "passing" by genuinely failing the relational check rather than by being skipped). 677 tests passing (up from 676), zero regressions. Verified live against the real running driver, real bundled `mudlib/`, reproducing the plural-direct/singular-indirect case end to end with the same real per-candidate filtering the regression test proves. See STATUS.md's own entry for this slice for the full detail. Two real, confirmed bugs in real FluffOS 2.9's own `check_object_relations()` were found while porting and are ported faithfully rather than silently fixed or silently reproduced without comment, per this session's own explicit instruction: both flagged inline in `ParserPackage.cpp` with their real source citation: (1) a negative-ordinal indirect object (`"give the sword to the last guard"`) scans from the wrong starting bitvec index (real `BPI * state->num_matches`, not index 0); (2) the main pairing loop's own early-exit test uses `found_direct` for plain C truthiness instead of `found_direct >= 0` the way every other use of that same variable in that same real function does, confirmed inert for the ordinary no-ordinal case this slice targets (the loop simply runs to full completion instead of exiting early, changing nothing about the final result) but a real, confirmable bug regardless. See STATUS.md's own entry for this slice for the full ranking methodology and live verification. See the full component breakdown below for the complete real derivation of the rest of this row; the numbered items there remain accurate.

**Nicks remaining work (recorded 2026-08-19, not implemented this session, not trivial):** this is `parse_sentence()`'s own 4th argument, a mapping of nickname word to object. It is not `add_action` nickname-list handling. Real `f_parse_sentence()` (`packages/parser.c:3046-3050`) stores `parse_nicks` (4th arg) then `parse_env` (3rd). Real `add_nicknames()` (`parser.c:1095-1108`) marks hash entries `HV_NICKNAME` for string keys. Real `load_objects()` calls it at `parser.c:1162-1163` after the `"my"` adjective. Real `expand_node()` (`parser.c:1302-1323`) does a lazy lookup and only sets `HV_NOUN` if the object is already in `loaded_objects` (nicks alias existing objects, they do not add new ones). Real `parse_obj()` calls `expand_node()` at `parser.c:1402-1403` when `HV_NICKNAME` is set. This port: `HashEntry::isNickname` exists but is never set; `parseObj()` has no `isNickname`/`expand_node` path; `EfunTable.cpp`'s `parse_sentence` still does not pass a 4th arg into `ParserPackage::parseSentence()`. Corpus: Dead Souls/Lima call `parse_sentence(cmd)` or `parse_sentence(cmd, dbg)` only. The 3rd `env` argument is closed: `loadObjects()` already walked an explicit array, this session wired `parse_sentence()`'s third arg through and proved it with `testParseSentenceExplicitEnvArrayOverridesTheOrdinaryEnvironmentWalk`.

**Nicks implemented, 2026-08-20 (a further session):** re-read all three
cited real sections directly before building anything, not trusted
blind. `add_nicknames()` (`packages/parser.c:1095-1108`) confirmed
exactly as the note above already had it, plus one detail worth pinning
down since the note above did not: `mn->values[0]` is the mapping
node's own KEY slot, not a value column, confirmed against real
`mapping.h`'s own `mapping_node_t::values[2]` and `mapping.c:39`'s own
`MAP_SVAL_HASH(mn->values[0])` (hashing a node by its own key, the only
way a mapping's own lookup works at all), so "for string keys" in the
note above is exactly right, not ambiguous between key and value the
way the bare C read alone could be. `expand_node()` re-read in full,
four lines longer than the prior citation (`:1302-1323`, not `:1302-1319`
-- the extra lines are its own closing brace and the function-end, not
missed logic). Two real quirks confirmed and ported faithfully, flagged
inline in the code rather than silently smoothed over: the
`he->flags &= ~HV_NICKNAME;` clear is unconditional, the function's own
first statement, before any success/failure check, a *failed*
resolution permanently disables re-attempting that same hash entry for

`find_string_in_mapping()` (`mapping.c:830-848`) never returns null, it
returns `&const0u` on a missing key, so real code's own single
`sv->type != T_OBJECT` check already covers both "key missing" and "key
present but not an object", ported here as the two real cases it
actually is, since this driver's own `Mapping` has no equivalent
sentinel to reuse.

category as `get_bb_uid()`/`multiple_adj()`/`err_obs()` already found
dead elsewhere in this row's own investigation, not a port target,

2 new regression tests (`test/test_lexer.cpp`):
`testParseSentenceNicknameResolvesToAnAlreadyLoadedObject` (a nickname
word that is not any real noun id of the target object at all resolves
correctly when the object is reachable, same room as the player) and
`testParseSentenceNicknamePresentButObjectNotYetLoadedDoesNotResolve`
(the identical nickname mapping to the identical real, live, non-
destructed object, but the object sits in an unrelated, unreachable
room: correctly does not resolve, silently, not an error). 704 tests
passing (up from 702), zero regressions.

**Verified live against the real running driver, real bundled
`mudlib/`** (a scratch config on spare port 4132, a real Python TCP
client, four temporary scratch objects, two rooms, one item, one
player with real `can_get_obj`/`direct_get_obj`/`do_get_obj` callbacks
and its own `parse_add_rule("get", "OBJ")`, removed afterward, no
config or master changes): a real `eval`-driven `parse_sentence("get
sam", 0, 0, (["sam": widget]))` call, where `"sam"` is not `widget`'s
own real noun id at all, correctly resolved to the real `widget` object
when it was in the same room as the player (`r == 1`,
`probe() == widget`); the identical nickname mapping to the identical
real object, moved to a second, unreachable room, correctly did not
resolve (`r == 0`, `probe()` stayed its own default-initialized `0`,
never touched); and, confirming backward compatibility, the same player
object with no nicks argument at all still correctly failed to resolve
`"sam"` (`0`, since it really is not `widget`'s own noun id) while still
correctly resolving `"get widget"` via its own real, unrelated noun id
(`1`). One real, self-inflicted test-authoring mistake caught and
diagnosed correctly before trusting anything, not a driver bug: an
early attempt called bare `parse_sentence()` directly from inside the
`eval` body itself, whose own scratch object was never `parse_init()`'d
-- threw the real, correct `"is not known by the parser"` error, an
uncaught dispatch error that correctly dropped only that one triggering
connection, confirmed via the driver's own log and a follow-up `eval
return 500+1;` on a fresh connection returning `501` immediately after,
matching this project's own already-documented "an uncaught dispatch
error drops only the triggering connection" behavior, not a regression.
Scratch object files removed before stopping the scratch process,
confirmed via `git status` that `mudlib/` is exactly as found.

**Full real component breakdown** (confirmed by reading the entire real source, `packages/parser.c` + `packages/parser.h`, not assumed from the file's size alone): written when the package was greenlit, kept here unedited as history; see the update note immediately above for current status:

1. **The rule-string tokenizer** (`tokenize()`/`make_rule()`, parser.c:653-768): turns a rule string like `"OBJ to LIV"` into an int array of tokens (`OBJ_A_TOKEN`/`LIV_A_TOKEN`/`OBS_TOKEN`/`LVS_TOKEN`/`STR_TOKEN`/`WRD_TOKEN`, each optionally OR'd with `l`/`v`/`p`/`c` modifier bits via an `"OBJ:lvpc"` suffix syntax) or a negative index into a master-supplied literal-word table. Pure string-to-int-array logic given a literal list; genuinely self-contained. Includes a real, confirmed-faithfully-reproduced off-by-one: a rule using exactly all 10 of `MAX_MATCHES`'s slots always errors "only 10 tokens permitted", even when the 10th token really was the rule's last one (real `make_rule()`'s own loop-exit check runs before it ever gets a chance to notice the input ran out): dead-letter in practice (this driver's own corpus survey found nothing longer than 3 tokens) but kept exactly as real, not silently "fixed". **Implemented this session**, `ParserPackage::tokenizeRule()`.
2. **The rule-string inverse stringifier** (`rule_string()`, parser.c:1691-1738): the exact inverse of (1), used by `parse_dump()` and (later) error messages. **Implemented this session**, `ParserPackage::ruleString()`.
3. **Master literal/preposition interrogation, the rule-registration half** (`interrogate_master()`'s `MS_HAS_LITERALS` branch only, parser.c:422-441): one master apply, `parse_command_prepos_list()`, confirmed directly against the real vendored `applies` source file that the macro name `APPLY_LITERALS` is genuinely misleading, it is specifically the mudlib's own legal-preposition-word list, nothing more general. **Implemented this session** (`EfunTable.cpp`'s `parse_add_rule` registration), deliberately narrowed to just this one apply, the same real function's `MS_HAS_USERS`/`MS_HAS_SPECIALS` halves (master's own `parse_command_users()` apply, plus the `the`/`me`/`myself`/`all`/`of`/ordinal special-word table) are purely about sentence matching, not rule registration, and stay unported until slice 6 below needs them. No `parse_refresh()`-driven cache invalidation yet either (real `master_state` bits): this slice's own literal fetch just always re-applies, harmless since nothing calls it more than once per rare, setup-time `parse_add_rule()` call.
4. **The verb/rule registry** (`verb_t`/`verb_node_t`/`verb_syn_t`, parser.h:133-166, plus `f_parse_add_rule`/`f_parse_remove`/`f_parse_dump`, parser.c:3170-3419 sans the synonym function): a table from verb name to a linked list of grammar rules, each tagged with its registering object (real `verb_node_t::handler`). **Implemented this session** as `ParserPackage` (`include/amlp/efun/ParserPackage.hpp` + `src/efun/ParserPackage.cpp`), one `unordered_map<string, VerbEntry>` (real code's own hash-bucket chaining collapsed to a single map keyed by `real_name`, a pure implementation-detail simplification with no observable contract), global process-wide static state deliberately mirroring real `parser.c`'s own single-game-per-process `verbs[]` global (matching this codebase's own established shape for exactly this kind of state, `object/LivingNameRegistry.hpp`). `VerbEntry`/`VerbRuleNode` both already carry `isSynonym`/`synonymOf` fields unused by this slice, so slice 5 below needs no struct rework, only a new `addSynonym()` method. `parse_dump()`'s own two deliberate, documented differences from real output: alphabetical verb order (real order is an unspecified `DO_HASH()` bit-mixing artifact, never a documented contract) and a destructed handler prints `"(destructed)"` rather than dereferencing a dangling pointer (real `parse_free()`, called from `free_object()`, unlinks a destructed handler's own rules immediately, not ported yet, see slice 7). `parse_remove()` deliberately does not replicate real code's own accidental UB when a synonym entry is reached through it (`verb_syn_t` has no `node` field at the offset real code reads one from), not reachable yet anyway, since slice 5 (`parse_add_synonym`) is the only thing that can ever create a synonym entry.
5. **`parse_add_synonym()` (both its 2-arg alias form and 3-arg specific-rule-copy form)** (parser.c:3280-3391): verb-name aliasing (`match_name`/`real_name` divergence, `VB_IS_SYN`) plus, in the 3-arg form, copying one already-registered rule node from one verb to another with an ownership check (`vn->handler != current_object` errors). Reuses (1)/(4) entirely, no new infrastructure: the natural next slice. Not attempted this session to keep the first slice strictly to "tokenizer + registry, no sentence matching," per this row's own greenlight scoping.
6. **The sentence tokenizer** (`parse_sentence()`'s own word-splitter plus `parse_recurse()`'s combinatorial nickname-merging, parser.c:2822-2985): splits raw player input into `words[]` (punctuation/apostrophe stripping via `isignore()`, lowercasing), then recursively tries merging consecutive words into a single shared-string token so multi-word nicknames (`"battle axe"`) can match one `WRD`/noun slot. A genuinely different tokenizer from (1): different job, different code, no reuse between them.
7. **`parse_init()`/`parse_refresh()`'s fuller `parse_info_t`** (parser.h:79-101, parser.c:472-571): this session ported only the single "has `parse_init()` been called" bit (`LpcObject::hasParseInfo()`). Real `parse_info_t` is a full per-object cache of noun/adjective/plural id lists (from the `APPLY_NOUN`/`APPLY_PLURAL`/`APPLY_ADJECTIVE` applies), `is_living`/`inventory_accessible`/`inventory_visible` flags, and `PI_REFRESH`/`PI_VERB_HANDLER`/`PI_REMOTE_LIVINGS` bookkeeping, invalidated on demand by `parse_refresh()`. Also includes real `parse_free()` (called from `free_object()`), which unlinks a destructed handler's own verb-registry rule nodes immediately: this session's slice instead leaves them in place and has `parse_dump()` print `"(destructed)"` for them (see slice 4's own note); a genuinely correct and sufficient behavior for a registry-only slice, but real `parse_free()`'s eager cleanup becomes worth porting once sentence matching actually iterates live rule nodes.
8. **The noun-phrase-to-object resolution engine** (`load_objects()`/`add_to_hash_table()`/`parse_obj()`, parser.c:779-1543): walks a starting object's environment/inventory (matching real `is_living`/`inventory_accessible`/`inventory_visible` semantics from (7)) to build the numbered object universe for one `parse_sentence()` call, a shared hash table mapping each noun/plural/adjective word to a bitvector of which numbered objects respond to it, then matches individual sentence words against it (ordinals, "the second sword" --, `"all"`/`"all of"`, `"my"`, nicknames via a caller-supplied mapping). The single largest, most novel remaining piece, no reusable existing infrastructure in this driver (bitvectors over up to 1024 objects, environment/inventory traversal already exists via `LpcObject::environment()`/`inventory()` but nothing else here does).
9. **The recursive-descent rule matcher and ambiguity-resolution engine** (`parse_rule()`/`parse_rules()`/`we_are_finished()`/`do_the_call()` plus the whole `check_object_relations()`/`singular_check_functions()`/`plural_check_functions()`/`dependent_check_functions()`/`parallel_check_functions()`/`make_function()` family, parser.c:1670-2636 and 2638-2812): backtracking grammar matching against a verb's registered rule nodes, weight-based best-match tracking, then the real `can_verb_*`/`direct_verb_*`/`indirect_verb_*`/`do_verb_*` callback-name convention invoked on matched objects to resolve two-object-rule ambiguity and finally commit to one interpretation. The most FluffOS-specific, deeply object-callback-coupled part of the whole package: needs (6) and (8) as prerequisites.
10. **Error reporting** (`parser_error_t`/`get_the_error()`/`make_error_message()`, parser.c:1545-1595 and 2987-3028): the full error taxonomy (`ERR_IS_NOT`/`ERR_NOT_LIVING`/`ERR_NOT_ACCESSIBLE`/`ERR_AMBIG`/`ERR_ORDINAL`/`ERR_THERE_IS_NO`/`ERR_ALLOCATED`/`ERR_BAD_MULTIPLE`/`ERR_MANY_PATHS`), surfaced back through `parse_sentence()`'s return value via `master()->parse_error_message()`. Needed once (9) exists to have anything to report errors about.
11. **The efun surface itself, remaining names**: `parse_refresh()` (needs 7), `parse_sentence()`/`parse_my_rules()` (need 6, 8, 9, 10 all present: these are the two names that actually run a sentence through the whole pipeline).

**Why this session's first slice was (1)+(2)+(3)+(4), i.e. `parse_init`/`parse_add_rule`/`parse_dump`/`parse_remove`, confirmed from real source rather than assumed:** this is genuinely the smallest real, buildable-in-one-session, non-throwaway slice: it needs no sentence tokenizer (6), no noun-phrase resolution engine (8, the two hardest remaining pieces), and no live-object callback machinery (9), yet is still real, complete, byte-faithful-where-it-matters ported code exercising the actual `packages/parser.c` grammar (including its own real off-by-one, see (1)), not a stub. `parse_refresh()` was left out even though it looks small: its own real behavior is entirely about invalidating the noun/adj/plural caches (7) and the master literal/user caches (3's still-unported two-thirds) that this slice doesn't have yet, so implementing it now would either be a no-op with nothing to invalidate or would need to reach ahead into slice 7's scope prematurely.

**What was built:** `include/amlp/efun/ParserPackage.hpp` + `src/efun/ParserPackage.cpp` (tokenizer, rule stringifier, verb/rule registry), a new `LpcObject::hasParseInfo()`/`setHasParseInfo()` flag (real `object_t::pinfo`'s "has `parse_init()` been called" bit only), and four new `EfunTable.cpp` registrations (`parse_init`, `parse_add_rule`, `parse_remove`, `parse_dump`). 6 new regression tests (`test/test_lexer.cpp`): the `parse_init()`-required guard; a plain-`OBJ`-rule round trip through `parse_dump()` plus `parse_init()`'s own idempotency; grammar rejection (more than two object tokens, more than one plural token); modifier/literal tokenizing against a real master `parse_command_prepos_list()` apply (`"OBJ to LVS:v"`) plus rejecting a word not in that list; `parse_remove()` only ever touching the calling object's own rule nodes, not another object's registration under the same verb; and a destructed handler's rule surfacing as `"(destructed)"` in `parse_dump()` rather than a dangling reference. 640 tests passing (up from 634), zero regressions. **Verified live against the real running driver, real bundled `mudlib/`** (a scratch config on a spare port, real telnet-negotiating client, real `eval` calls, the shipped mudlib's own `mudlib/command/eval.c` recreates a fresh `/tmp_eval_file` object on every separate `eval` invocation, so registry state tied to caller identity had to be exercised within one combined `eval` call, not split across several): `parse_init(); parse_add_rule("eat","OBJ"); parse_add_rule("give","OBJ LIV"); parse_dump()` returned the correct two-verb dump with the correct handler and rule-string text; `parse_remove("eat")` then removed only that verb's own rule node, confirmed via a second `parse_dump()` in the same call; `parse_add_rule()` called before `parse_init()` threw the intended guard error (wrapped in `catch()` purely so one dropped connection didn't interrupt the rest of the same client session, an uncaught dispatch error correctly closes only the triggering connection, `net_dead()` firing on it exactly as ordinary link death does; it does not take down the driver process, rigorously re-confirmed this same session with a second, independent, continuously-connected client plus 60+ seconds of active polling after the trigger, see STATUS.md's own "Crash-claim correction" section: an earlier draft of this note wrongly repeated a stale "drops the connection and the driver process" claim that a session between the original `m_indices`/`m_values` work and this one had already investigated and retracted); and `"OBJ OBJ OBJ"` was correctly rejected as a grammar error, all against the real, unmodified mudlib.

**Second real slice landed (2026-08-18, continued further): `parse_add_synonym()`, item (5) above, both real forms.** Confirmed from source that it genuinely reused (1)/(2)/(4) as expected, with one real correction to (4)'s own registry shape found along the way: real `parse_sentence()`'s own verb-lookup loop does not stop at the first match, so a single verb name can carry *more than one* `VerbEntry` at once (e.g. a plain rule-holding entry and, separately, a synonym-to-something-else entry, both named identically), confirmed directly against the real source, not assumed. This session's own prior slice had (harmlessly, since nothing exercised it yet) modeled the registry as one entry per name; corrected to a map from name to a *list* of entries before implementing `parse_add_synonym()`, rather than building the new feature on a known-wrong shape the very next sentence-matching slice would only have had to fix later anyway. Implemented: `ParserPackage::addSynonym()` (`include/amlp/efun/ParserPackage.hpp`/`.cpp`), the 2-arg alias form (`syn->flags = VB_IS_SYN; syn->real = vb;`, a fresh or reused entry keyed by `new_verb` with `match_name==old_verb`) and the 3-arg rule-copy form (tokenizes `rule`, finds the one already-registered node under `old_verb` whose token sequence matches exactly, checks its handler is the calling object, real "Rule owned by different object.", then copies that one node onto a fresh or reused non-synonym entry). Both forms require `old_verb` to already name a real, non-synonym verb (real "%s is not a verb!", with one real driver-internal FluffOS quirk deliberately not replicated: real code's own separate shared-string-interning null check, which this driver has no equivalent global string table to reproduce, the one real lookup-failure check that actually matters is the only one needed here) and reject `new_verb == old_verb` (real "Verb cannot be a synonym for itself."). `f_parse_add_rule()`'s own literal-fetch block was pulled out into a shared `fetchParserLiterals()` helper (`EfunTable.cpp`) since `parse_add_synonym()`'s 3-arg form needs the exact same master `parse_command_prepos_list()` apply to tokenize its own `rule` argument. 3 new regression tests: the alias form's own `parse_dump()` shape (`"Verb new (old):\n  Synonym for: old\n"`); the rule-copy form copying only the one matching rule (not a different rule also registered under the same verb) plus its three real rejection paths (wrong owner, no such rule, self-synonym, unregistered old verb); and the coexisting-plain-and-synonym-entry-under-one-name scenario the registry refactor exists for, including confirming `parse_remove()` only ever touches the plain entry, never the synonym one sharing its name. 643 tests passing (up from 640), zero regressions. **Verified live against the real running driver, real bundled `mudlib/`** (same telnet-and-`eval` methodology as the first slice, this time via this harness's own tracked `run_in_background`/`TaskStop` mechanism rather than manual shell backgrounding, see STATUS.md's own "Crash-claim correction" entry for why that distinction matters): both forms of `parse_add_synonym()` produced the exact expected `parse_dump()` text; the self-synonym and no-such-rule rejections fired correctly; and, live and unprompted, a real, faithful (not a bug) quirk surfaced, a *failed* 3-arg `parse_add_synonym()` call still leaves behind an empty target `VerbEntry` (real code's own verb-entry-creation step runs before the rule-lookup that can fail, and nothing rolls it back on error, matching this whole file's general non-transactional error style elsewhere), confirmed to match real code's own control flow exactly rather than being a bug in this port.

**Third real slice landed (2026-08-18, continued further): item (7), narrowed to its genuinely separable half after confirming the rest from source.** Asked to consider whether (7) really unblocks the most of what is left before defaulting to it, re-read `interrogate_object()` (packages/parser.c:779-842, the function that actually populates real `parse_info_t`'s noun/adjective/plural id cache) and confirmed it has exactly one real call site, `load_objects()` (packages/parser.c:1146), itself only reachable from `parse_sentence()`, the cache-*population* half of (7) is not separable from (8) at all, contrary to what "parse_info_t/parse_refresh()/parse_free() before the harder pieces" could be read to suggest at face value. What genuinely *is* separable, confirmed the same way: real `parse_free()` (called from `free_object()`, unlinking a destructed handler's own rule nodes) and real `f_parse_refresh()`'s own flag/apply mechanics (the `PI_SETUP`/`PI_REFRESH`/`PI_VERB_HANDLER` bit dance, the master-object special case, and the `LIVINGS_ARE_REMOTE` apply/`PI_REMOTE_LIVINGS` flag), neither touches the noun/adj/plural cache itself, and both are fully real, testable, and observable without (6) or (8). This also surfaced a genuine gap in the first slice's own `parse_add_rule()`: real code also fires `LIVINGS_ARE_REMOTE` and sets `PI_VERB_HANDLER`/`PI_REMOTE_LIVINGS` there (packages/parser.c:3267-3273), deliberately deferred at the time as "purely for sentence-matching", corrected this slice now that the flag storage it needed exists.

Implemented: real `parse_info_t::flags` as `LpcObject::parseInfoFlags()` (an `int`, valid only while `hasParseInfo()` is true, matching real "pinfo != 0"; the real `PI_*` bit values live in a new `ParserInfoFlag` namespace, `ParserPackage.hpp`), the per-object noun/adj/plural id arrays real `parse_info_t` also carries stay unported, since nothing before (8) populates or reads them. `ParserPackage::onObjectDestroyed()` (real `parse_free()`): if the destructed object was ever `PI_VERB_HANDLER`, walks every verb entry in the registry unlinking its own rule nodes, then clears its own `hasParseInfo()`/`parseInfoFlags()` (real "FREE(pinfo);"); wired into both `destruct` and `reload_object`'s existing `onDestructed` callback (the same real trigger point and cascade-safe shape `SocketRegistry::closeAllOwnedBy()` already uses there). The `parse_refresh` efun (new registration, `EfunTable.cpp`): the guard error for an object that never called `parse_init()`, the master-object special case (a silent no-op even without `parse_init()`), the real flag-preserving-`PI_VERB_HANDLER`-only bit manipulation, and the `LIVINGS_ARE_REMOTE` re-check with real code's own `O_DESTRUCTED` guard after the apply. `parse_add_rule()` itself: now also sets `PI_VERB_HANDLER` and fires the same apply/flag check (without the `O_DESTRUCTED` guard, matching real code's own asymmetry between the two call sites exactly, confirmed a real, harmless omission in the original FluffOS source, not invented here, and harmless in this driver's own memory model too since `LpcObject` is never actually freed on destruct).

4 new regression tests: the real, eager `parse_free()` cleanup via the actual `destruct()` efun (distinct from the existing "(destructed)" fallback test, which deliberately calls the lower-level `VM::destructObject()` directly with no callback, exercising a real gap this driver's own reference-counted memory model can hit that genuine FluffOS never could: both scenarios are now separately covered and both `dump()` outcomes are real and intentional, not one right and one stale); the `parse_refresh()` guard error plus the real master-object special case; and both `parse_add_rule()` and `parse_refresh()` firing `LIVINGS_ARE_REMOTE` and setting `PI_REMOTE_LIVINGS` only when the apply actually returns truthy. 646 tests passing (up from 643), zero regressions. **Verified live against the real running driver, real bundled `mudlib/`** (same harness-tracked `run_in_background`/`TaskStop` methodology as the previous slice): a real cloned object's rule vanished from `parse_dump()` entirely (not merely marked destructed) immediately after a real `destruct()` call on it; a real `livings_are_remote()` call-counter confirmed the apply fires exactly once from `parse_add_rule()` and a second time from a subsequent `parse_refresh()`; and the ordinary no-`parse_init()` guard fired correctly via `catch()`. Driver process stayed healthy throughout.

**Fourth real slice landed (2026-08-18, continued further): `parse_sentence()` itself, items (6)+(9)+(10) restricted to STR/WRD/literal-only rules: no OBJ/LIV/OBS/LVS support yet.** Asked explicitly to confirm from source whether (6)+(8)+(9) together (the previously-assumed "smallest remaining group") was really the only option, or whether some smaller piece inside that group was separable on its own. Traced every real call path into `interrogate_object()` (the function that would populate a live object's own noun/adjective/plural id cache, item 8's own core) and confirmed its *only* real caller is `load_objects()`, itself only reachable from `parse_obj()`, itself only reached by `parse_rule()`'s own OBJ/LIV/OBS/LVS token case: a rule built entirely from STR tokens, WRD tokens, and literal words never reaches any of that machinery at all, in real FluffOS included, not just in a scoped-down port of it. This makes "STR/WRD/literal-only `parse_sentence()`" a genuine subset of real behavior, not a simplified stand-in for it: every function this slice ported (the word-splitter, the recursive-descent matcher, the can_/direct_/indirect_/do_ callback machinery, error reporting) is the exact real function, with the *unreachable* OBJ-family branches left in as explicit, documented dead code (an obvious insertion point for the next slice) rather than silently omitted.

Confirmed real component pieces, each independently verified against source before porting (not assumed from the earlier scoping pass's own summary): the word-splitter (real `parse_sentence()`'s own tokenizer, item 6) and the multi-word-verb-phrase lookup (real code's own progressive `findstring()`-merge technique, replicated here via a plain registry lookup since this driver has no global string-interning table); the recursive-descent matcher (`parse_rule()`/`parse_rules()`/`we_are_finished()`, item 9), restricted as above; the callback machinery (`check_functions()`/`process_answer()`/`make_function()`/`push_real_names()`/`do_the_call()`, also item 9), `make_function()`'s own four real naming *strategies* (embed the verb's own name directly; the same but push literal-token arguments as data; a generic "verb" name with the verb's own name pushed as an argument instead; the whole name collapsed to "do_verb_rule" with the exact rule string pushed as an argument) ported in full, all four confirmed genuinely reachable and correct via a dedicated live/regression test for the least-obvious one (the fourth, "do_verb_rule" form, an easy one to get wrong and the one this session's own regression test caught nothing on, first try, only after very careful line-by-line re-derivation of the real 5-argument shape it produces); error reporting (`make_error_message()`/`get_the_error()`, item 10), restricted to the one real error kind (`ERR_ALLOCATED`, a can_/direct_/... callback's own explicit string rejection) this slice's own matcher can actually produce, every other real error kind (`ERR_IS_NOT`/`ERR_NOT_LIVING`/`ERR_NOT_ACCESSIBLE`/`ERR_AMBIG`/`ERR_ORDINAL`/`ERR_THERE_IS_NO`/`ERR_BAD_MULTIPLE`/`ERR_MANY_PATHS`) is exclusively produced by object-token matching, confirmed unreachable the same way, and listed with its real numeric value (`include/parser_error.h`, read directly) so the next slice extends the same enum rather than renumbering it. `f_parse_sentence()`'s own real two required arguments (`env`/`nicks`, the third and fourth) are accepted for real signature compatibility but never consulted, since both are exclusively read by `load_objects()`.

One real, genuinely new registry-shape requirement found and added cleanly on top of the existing structure (not a rework, confirmed before writing anything): real `VB_HAS_OBJ` needed a *per-rule*, not per-verb, home for this slice's own purposes (deciding which individual rule nodes to skip, not just which verbs to bother loading objects for): added as `VerbRuleNode::hasObjectToken`, computed once at `addRule()`/rule-copy time from the already-tokenized rule, with zero changes to anything built in the three earlier slices.

One real, driver-wide gap found and fixed along the way, orthogonal to parsing itself: `process_answer()`'s own three-way `T_NUMBER`/`T_STRING`/anything-else branch needs to treat a defined function's *implicit* "no explicit `return` statement" result as real LPC's own implicit `return 0;` (a falsy number, triggering the same "explicit rejection, record an error" path a literal `return 0;` would), but this driver's own `Return` opcode represents that exact case as `Value{}` (monostate/"void"), not a real `int64_t 0` (`VM.cpp`'s own `"if (localStack.empty()) return Value{};"`, confirmed directly, not assumed), which would otherwise make a genuinely-defined-but-early-falling-off can_/direct_/do_ function get treated the same as an undefined one. Fixed by checking `VM::functionExists()` explicitly *before* the call (already needed anyway, to distinguish "undefined, try the next naming convention" from "defined, whatever it returned") and then treating monostate the same as an explicit falsy int64_t 0 in `process_answer()`'s own port specifically, a targeted fix scoped to this efun family, not a change to the driver-wide monostate/int-0 representation choice itself, which several other real call sites elsewhere in this codebase already rely on staying as-is.

One real, deliberate architecture improvement over real code, documented rather than silently made: real `parse_sentence()`'s own "current parse in progress" state (`num_words`/`words[]`/`matches[]`/`parse_vn`/`best_match`/etc) is a set of global C statics, and real code's own recursion guard against a nested `parse_sentence()` call is explicitly disabled ("may not be done in case of an error, or in case of tail recursion", real code's own comment): meaning a genuinely reentrant call in real FluffOS can silently corrupt an outer parse still in progress. This driver's own port collects the same state into one `SentenceSession` object constructed fresh per call and threaded through by reference instead of reintroducing the same global-mutable-state hazard in C++; not a fidelity loss, since nothing any real caller depends on requires the corruption itself, only each call's own correct result, which is identical either way for any single, non-reentrant call (still the only kind any of this row's own tests exercise).

10 new regression tests (`test/test_lexer.cpp`): a basic STR match invoking `do_<verb>_str` under the simplest real naming convention; a WRD match (exactly one word); a literal-plus-STR rule against a real master `parse_command_prepos_list()` apply; no verb recognized at all (returns `0`); a verb recognized but the grammar unsatisfied (returns `-1`, real `-found_level`); an explicit string rejection correctly routed through a real master `parser_error_message()` apply (confirmed the master genuinely received `ERR_ALLOCATED`'s own real numeric value, `6`); the same rejection falling back to a plain `0` when master does not define that apply; an `OBJ`-token rule coexisting with a sibling `STR`-token rule under the same verb, confirming the `OBJ` one is skipped gracefully rather than breaking the whole verb; the "do_verb_rule" generic-naming fallback (the most intricate real path in the whole slice, all 5 of its own real arguments individually confirmed); and both the `parse_init()` guard and the debug-flag guard (a truthy second argument correctly throws the real "Parser debugging not enabled" error, matching real code's own non-`DEBUG`-build behavior exactly, since this driver has no parser-debug tracing at all). 656 tests passing (up from 646), zero regressions.

**Verified live against the real running driver, real bundled `mudlib/`** (same harness-tracked `run_in_background`/`TaskStop` methodology as the previous two slices): a real cloned object's `parse_add_rule("look","STR")` plus `parse_sentence("look at the mysterious door")` returned `1` and correctly invoked `do_look_str()` with the real original-cased text `"at the mysterious door"`; an unrecognized verb (`"xyzzy nonsense"`) returned `0`; a recognized verb with unsatisfiable grammar (`"frobnicate"` alone against a `"STR"`-only rule) returned `-1`; an explicit string rejection from `can_eat_str()` correctly fell back to `0` since the real bundled master does not define `parser_error_message()`; and ordinary gameplay (`eval return 6*7;`, `who`) kept working throughout.

**One real, incidental, unrelated finding surfaced during this session's own live verification, not caused by this slice and not fixed here:** the driver process has no `SIGPIPE` handling at all (confirmed directly: `grep -rn "SIGPIPE" src/ include/` returns zero hits, and `main.cpp` registers handlers only for `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`), a client connection closing at the wrong moment relative to the driver's own next `write()`/`send()` to that same socket can raise `SIGPIPE`, whose default disposition terminates the entire process, not just that one connection, for every player. Reproduced once, live, during this session's own test-client cleanup (a background driver instance exited with code 141, the standard `128+SIGPIPE` shell convention, immediately after a two-command client session closed its socket), not reliably reproducible on demand (a second, identical attempt did not retrigger it, consistent with this being a real timing-dependent race rather than a deterministic bug), but the underlying gap itself (no `signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN)`, no `MSG_NOSIGNAL`/`SO_NOSIGPIPE` anywhere) is confirmed with certainty directly from source, independent of how reliably any one test happened to trigger it. A different root cause from the already-resolved "uncaught LPC exception" crash-claim earlier this same session (STATUS.md's own "Crash-claim correction" entry), that one was a C++ exception-handling question with a confirmed-correct answer (no crash); this one is a raw OS signal with no handling at all, genuinely reproducible in principle by any real player's abrupt disconnect, not specific to this row's own `parse_*` work, and out of this session's own scope to fix (a `src/net`-level concern). Flagged here with its exact real citation so a future session does not need to rediscover it.

**Item 8 (noun-phrase-to-object resolution engine) investigated fresh, same session as the SIGPIPE fix (2026-08-18, continued further): confirmed genuinely too large to force into the same session, real scope re-derived and broken into its own sub-pieces below so the next session can start building immediately rather than re-reading `load_objects()`/`parse_obj()` from scratch.** Re-read `load_objects()` (packages/parser.c:1110-1191) and `parse_obj()` (:1325-1543) directly, with fresh eyes, rather than trusting the two-sessions-old scoping note's own summary. Confirmed: unlike `parse_sentence()`'s own STR/WRD/literal subset (this row's own immediately preceding slice), there is no genuinely smaller separable piece inside item 8: even the single simplest possible `OBJ` match (`parse_add_rule("get", "OBJ")`, "get sword") needs the *entire* real chain below working end to end before `parse_obj()` (the actual word-matching logic) has anything real to operate on at all; none of the five sub-pieces is independently observable via any real efun surface the way the sentence tokenizer's own word-splitter turned out to almost be.

**Real sub-pieces, in the order `load_objects()` itself actually needs them:**

1. **The real per-object noun/adjective/plural cache** (`interrogate_object()`, packages/parser.c:779-842), six real applies per object interrogated (`parse_command_id_list`/`parse_command_plural_id_list`/`parse_command_adjectiv_id_list`/`is_living`/`inventory_accessible`/`inventory_visible`), populating real `parse_info_t::ids`/`adjs`/`plurals` (string arrays, not yet stored anywhere on `LpcObject` at all, the previous `parse_free()`/`parse_refresh()` slice deliberately left this exact gap, see `LpcObject::parseInfoFlags()`'s own comment) plus the real `PI_LIVING`/`PI_INV_ACCESSIBLE`/`PI_INV_VISIBLE` flags (storage already exists in `ParserInfoFlag`, from the same prior slice, genuinely unused until this piece populates them). A real, lazy, lockstep-with-`PI_SETUP`/`PI_REFRESH` cache, not a one-shot fetch: `parse_refresh()`'s own existing real behavior (already ported) becomes fully meaningful only once this exists to invalidate.
2. **The rest of `interrogate_master()`** (packages/parser.c:402-470, `MS_HAS_USERS`/`MS_HAS_SPECIALS` halves: the `MS_HAS_LITERALS` third is already real and in use since the first `parse_*` slice): master's own `users()` apply (the connected-player list) and the fixed special-word table (`the`/`me`/`myself`/`all`/`of`/ordinal words `first`..`ninth` plus numeric-ordinal parsing like "3rd"/"21st", real `check_special_word()`).
3. **Environment-based object collection** (`rec_add_object()`/`find_uninited_objects()`/`add_objects_from_array()`/`get_objects_from_array()`, packages/parser.c:844-990): the most tractable piece, since `LpcObject::environment()`/`inventory()` already exist and match real `super()`/`first_inv()`/`next_inv()` closely; still needs the real `RAO_INREACH`/`RAO_MY` propagation rules and the `PI_INV_VISIBLE`/`PI_INV_ACCESSIBLE` gating from piece 1 to walk correctly.
4. **The word-to-objects hash table** (`add_hash_entry()`/`add_to_hash_table()`/`bitvec_*`, packages/parser.c:226-326 and 992-1069), maps each noun/plural/adjective word to the *set* of object indices that respond to it, built from piece 1's own per-object cache. Real code's own fixed-size `bitvec_t` (a `MAX_NUM_OBJECTS`==1024-bit array) is a pure C-array sizing detail, not itself load-bearing semantics, a `std::vector<bool>`/`std::unordered_set<size_t>`-based equivalent is a reasonable, faithful modernization here, the same kind of clean substitution already used for the verb registry's own hash-bucket-to-map change.
5. **`parse_obj()` itself** (packages/parser.c:1325-1543, ~220 lines), the real word-matching logic: articles ("the"), "all"/"all of X", possessive "my X", ordinals ("the second sword", "the 3rd sword"), nicknames (via `parse_nicks`, `expand_node()`), adjective chains (`last_adj`/`multiple_adj`, "red X" narrowing before the noun itself is read), singular-vs-plural noun matching (`OBJ` vs `OBS`), and the `LIV_MODIFIER`/`VIS_ONLY_MODIFIER` filters, all via bitvector intersection against piece 4's own hash table. This is the piece `parseRule()`/`makeFunction()`/`makeErrorMessage()` in `ParserPackage.cpp` already have an exact, explicitly-commented dead-branch insertion point waiting for (this session's own `parse_sentence()` slice left them there deliberately).

**Recommended internal order for whoever picks this up:** 1 and 2 first (both are "just wire up more real applies," similar in shape to work already done this row, 2 in particular is a small, bounded, `interrogate_master()`-shaped addition on top of the already-real literals third); 3 next (mostly assembling existing `LpcObject` primitives); 4 as the new data-structure piece; 5 last, once everything it needs to operate on is real. Realistically too large for one sitting even with that ordering, 1+2+3+4 alone is comparable in scope to this row's entire `parse_sentence()` slice, and 5 is comparable in intricacy to `make_function()`'s own four-naming-strategy logic, on top of it, not instead of it. A first sub-slice landing just 1+2+3+4 (the object-collection-and-cache pipeline, confirmed real and complete on its own even with `parse_obj()` itself still a documented gap) is a reasonable place to stop for a single session, the same "half the group, cleanly bounded" pattern this row's own `parse_free()`/`parse_refresh()` slice already used successfully for item 7.

`check_object_relations()`/`singular_check_functions()`/`plural_check_functions()`/`dependent_check_functions()`/`parallel_check_functions()` (the two-object ambiguity-resolution family, also part of item 9's own real scope, packages/parser.c:2029-2493) is the natural follow-on after item 8 lands, not before it: none of it has anything to operate on without real object matches to disambiguate. `parse_my_rules()` (the remaining efun-surface item) is a thin variant of `parse_sentence()` itself and could reasonably be picked up any time, independent of item 8.

**Fifth real slice landed (2026-08-19, fresh session, evidence-based re-rank): item 8's own recommended first sub-slice, pieces 1+2+3+4 (the real per-object noun/adjective/plural cache, the rest of `interrogate_master()`, environment-based object collection, and the word -> object-index hash table): confirmed real, complete, and independently testable on their own, real `load_objects()` in full minus `add_nicknames()` and `parse_obj()` itself (piece 5, still open).** Re-ranked all four open candidates fresh at session start (this row's own remaining pieces vs. row 1.9 mapping width vs. row 1.7/1.8 LDMud closure kinds vs. row 1.4/1.16 connect/disconnect) against real mudlib-compatibility impact rather than size: this row already carries the strongest real-evidence citation on record (`temp/core-lib`'s own Dead Souls `lib/lib/command.c` calls `parse_sentence()` directly from core command dispatch, confirmed two sessions ago, not a peripheral feature) and, unlike mapping width or closure kinds, already has a concrete, citation-backed, right-sized next sub-slice on record from the immediately preceding session's own investigation (this row's own "Recommended internal order" paragraph above) rather than needing fresh scoping work before anything could be built. Connect/disconnect was reconsidered too, since DGD's own three-way port fork, the part of that design question that most complicated a `BootApi` interface change, is no longer a Phase 1 blocker at all (DGD is comparison-only per the Phase 1 header's own 2026-08-18 scope clarification), narrowing that row to just a FluffOS-vs-LDMud `net_dead()`-vs-`disconnect()` divergence; genuinely real, but narrower in reach than this row's own confirmed-highest-citation status, and still an open architecture question rather than a scoped, ready-to-build slice. Picked this row's own pieces 1-4 on that basis.

Confirmed real component pieces directly against source (not the prior session's own summary alone) before writing anything: `interrogate_object()` (packages/parser.c:779-842, piece 1), six real applies per object (`parse_command_id_list`/`parse_command_plural_id_list`/`parse_command_adjectiv_id_list`/`is_living`/`inventory_accessible`/`inventory_visible`, confirmed real apply *names* directly against `applies.h`, including the real, faithfully-kept "adjectiv" typo in `APPLY_ADJECTIVE`'s own name) plus the exact real `PI_SETUP`/`PI_REFRESH` cache-hit/re-interrogate flag dance and its own real early-return-after-each-apply checkpoints; the rest of `interrogate_master()` (packages/parser.c:402-470, piece 2), the `MS_HAS_USERS` half (`master()->parse_command_users()`, confirmed real apply name directly against `applies_table.c`'s own index-21 entry) and the `MS_HAS_SPECIALS` half (the fixed article/self/all/of/and/ordinal word table plus real numeric-ordinal parsing, including real code's own "a teen is always 'th'" rule); environment-based object collection (packages/parser.c:844-990, piece 3), `rec_add_object()`/`find_uninited_objects()`/`add_objects_from_array()`/`get_objects_from_array()`, all four, including a real, deliberate asymmetry between the first and third of these confirmed by close reading rather than assumed identical (`rec_add_object()`'s own `ob == parse_user` case sets `RAO_MY` for that object's own children; `add_objects_from_array()`'s own equivalent case does not propagate onto the same element's own recorded flags, only onto a nested array immediately following it, real `last_was_me`); the word -> object-index hash table (packages/parser.c:992-1069, piece 4), `add_hash_entry()`/`add_to_hash_table()`, plus real `load_objects()`'s own final "num_people" fallback loop (packages/parser.c:1165-1187, confirmed via `f_parse_sentence()`, packages/parser.c:3070, that `parse_user` is unconditionally `current_object` regardless of whether an explicit `parse_env` argument is also given, so this fallback loop's own use of `super(parse_user)` is real and unconditional, not gated on the `parse_env` branch the way an initial reading of the surrounding code might suggest).

One real, deliberate architecture decision, called out rather than made silently: real `interrogate_master()`'s own `MS_HAS_USERS` cache genuinely spans multiple separate `parse_sentence()` calls (invalidated only by an explicit `parse_refresh()` on master), a real, observable mudlib-visible caching *contract*, not an internal-only performance detail, unlike `parse_sentence()`'s own prior `SentenceSession` improvement (collecting real global mutable statics into one fresh-per-call object, a pure implementation-detail simplification with no observable behavior change), collapsing this cache into a fresh-per-call structure would be a real fidelity loss: a mudlib that logs in a second player and expects an immediate `parse_sentence()` call to see them without calling `parse_refresh()` first would behave differently. Kept as real process-wide state (`ParserPackage`'s own `masterUsersCache()`, mirroring the already-established `cachedLiterals()` pattern), invalidated by the new `ParserPackage::invalidateMasterUsersCache()`, wired into the `parse_refresh` efun's own master-object branch (`EfunTable.cpp`) exactly where real code's own `master_state &= ~MS_HAS_USERS;` runs, unconditionally, before the "does master even have pinfo" early return, not after.

One real memory-management simplification, confirmed to have zero observable effect before relying on it: real `remove_ids()` (freeing a destructed/refreshing object's own stale `ids`/`plurals`/`adjs` C arrays before `interrogate_object()` overwrites them) is not ported at all, `LpcObject::setParseNounIds()`/etc. simply assign a fresh `std::vector<std::string>`, and C++'s own move-assignment already frees the old backing storage automatically. Confirmed this could not silently diverge from real behavior even memory-management-wise: real `remove_ids()`'s own guard (`if (pinfo->flags & PI_SETUP)`) is provably always false at the one real call site that matters (`interrogate_object()`'s own `PI_REFRESH` check) anyway, because real `f_parse_refresh()` clears `PI_SETUP` in the exact same bitwise AND that sets `PI_REFRESH`, meaning real `remove_ids()` never actually frees anything at that call site either, a genuine, confirmed real quirk in FluffOS's own source (a leaked `ids`/`plurals`/`adjs` array on every refresh, in the real C driver), not something this port needed to reproduce a C++ analog of.

Storage: three new `LpcObject` members (`parseNounIds_`/`parsePluralIds_`/`parseAdjIds_`, real `parse_info_t::ids`/`plurals`/`adjs`) plus accessors, `include/amlp/object/LpcObject.hpp`. New types in `ParserPackage.hpp`: `SpecialWordKind`/`SpecialWordResult` (real `SW_*`), `HashEntry` (real `hash_entry_t`, its three real fixed-size `bitvec_t` fields represented as `std::vector<bool>`, the same hash-bucket-chaining-to-map simplification already used for the verb registry), and `LoadedObjectSet` (real `loaded_objects[]`/`object_flags[]`/`cur_livings`/`cur_accessible`/`me_object`/`hash_table[]`, collected into one object built fresh per call and returned by value, the same deliberate `SentenceSession`-style architecture improvement already made for `parse_sentence()` itself, genuinely not a fidelity loss here either, since nothing in real `load_objects()` depends on its own global statics surviving a reentrant call any more than `parse_sentence()`'s own globals did). New `ParserPackage` static methods: `checkSpecialWord()`, `interrogateObject()`, `invalidateMasterUsersCache()`, `loadObjects()`.

**Not yet wired into `parseSentence()` itself**, item 8 piece 5 (`parse_obj()`, the real word-matching logic that would actually consume a `LoadedObjectSet` to resolve `OBJ`/`LIV`/`OBS`/`LVS` tokens against real objects) is the next slice, and remains the one piece with a concrete, already-marked insertion point (`ParserPackage.cpp`'s own documented `OBJ`-family dead branches in `parseRule()`/`makeFunction()`/`makeErrorMessage()`) but no implementation yet. This means pieces 1-4 have no real LPC-visible efun surface of their own yet either (real `load_objects()` has none in genuine FluffOS either, it is exclusively an internal implementation detail of `parse_sentence()`, never its own efun), so this slice's own live verification was necessarily narrower in scope than prior slices': confirmed the driver boots clean and stays healthy under 45 seconds of continuous active polling from a second, independent client while a first, disposable client ran ordinary `eval` gameplay (`6*7`, `m_indices()`, a `parse_init()`/`parse_add_rule()`/`parse_sentence()` round trip) throughout, with zero regressions: the strongest live confirmation available for a slice with no new LPC-visible surface of its own, the actual new pipeline's own correctness resting on 4 new regression tests instead (below), the same honest scoping already applied to `parse_free()`'s own eager-cleanup path when its own real trigger point had no direct efun surface either.

4 new regression tests (`test/test_lexer.cpp`): `checkSpecialWord()`'s own fixed table plus numeric-ordinal parsing, including the real "a teen is always 'th'" rule (`"11th"` correct, `"11st"` rejected, `"21st"` correct since 21 is not a teen); `interrogateObject()`'s own real cache-hit behavior (a second call does not re-invoke `parse_command_id_list()`) and real re-interrogation after `parse_refresh()` (confirmed via a live call counter); a full `loadObjects()` integration test building a real four-object environment tree (a room containing a player carrying a sword, plus a sibling rock) via real `move_object()` calls and confirming the exact real object numbering order, `me_object`, `RAO_INREACH`/`RAO_MY` propagation, the `cur_livings` bit, and the complete word -> object-index hash table (including the fixed `"my"` adjective covering exactly the player's own carried sword) all end up correct together, not piece by piece; and a combined master-users-caching-plus-`num_people`-fallback test confirming `master()->parse_command_users()` is genuinely reused across separate `loadObjects()` calls until an explicit `parse_refresh()` on master, and that a connected user sitting inside an `inventory_visible()`-false container (never reached by the ordinary tree walk) is still picked up by the real `num_people` fallback loop exactly once, not duplicated for a player the tree walk already found directly. 661 tests passing (up from 657), zero regressions.

**Sixth real slice landed (2026-08-19, fresh session): item 8 piece 5, `parse_obj()` itself (packages/parser.c:1325-1543), the real word-matching engine, wired into `parseSentence()` for the first time, plus the single-object slice of item 9's own can_/direct_/indirect_/do_ disambiguation family (`singular_check_functions()`/`plural_check_functions()`/`parallel_check_functions()`, packages/parser.c:1670-2157) needed to turn a `parse_obj()` match into an actually-resolved object and a real `do_*` call. OBJ/LIV/OBS/LVS rules now resolve real objects end to end for any rule with AT MOST ONE object-family token.**

Confirmed directly from source before writing anything (not assumed from the prior session's own scoping note): `parse_obj()` itself, articles ("the", real `continue`), "all"/"all of" (real `SW_ALL`, confirmed a bare "all" without a following "of" builds its match from the *current, unnarrowed* candidate bitvec right there rather than falling through to read a following noun word at all, "all swords" without "of" is NOT equivalent to "all of the swords" in real grammar, a genuine surprise caught while writing this session's own regression tests, not assumed), possessive "my" (already wired from the immediately preceding slice's own fixed hash entry), ordinals including real numeric parsing and the real "my first sword" ordinal-legality hack, adjective chains (`last_adj`, narrowing `objects` before the noun itself is read, `multiple_adj` confirmed write-only dead code in real source, not ported), singular-vs-plural noun matching (`HV_NOUN`/`HV_PLURAL`, both checked independently per real code, not mutually exclusive), and the `LIV_MODIFIER`/`VIS_ONLY_MODIFIER` filters via bitvector intersection against the previous slice's own `LoadedObjectSet` hash table. `expand_node()` (nickname resolution) is confirmed genuinely vacuous today, not silently skipped, `HashEntry::isNickname` is never set true by anything, since `add_nicknames()`/`parse_nicks` still has no real caller (this driver's own `parseSentence()` has no `nicks` parameter). `err_obs` confirmed write-only dead code in real source alongside `multiple_adj`, not ported either.

Wired into `parseRule()`'s own previously-documented-dead `OBJ`-family branch (calls `parse_obj()`, then the real "there is no X" fallback forward-search loop, `ERR_THERE_IS_NO`) and its own literal-mismatch recovery switch (reachable now for a rule shaped "OBJ <literal>", e.g. "give OBJ away", confirmed a real, faithfully-kept quirk that this recovery reads the *unmasked* previous token, below) and real `ERR_THERE_IS_NO` error-weight reduction. `make_function()`'s own OBJ-family branch implemented and reduced to the single-object case: confirmed directly from source (a genuine, `can_`/per-candidate `direct_`/`indirect_` probes always use "obj"/"liv" naming regardless of plurality, while only the final `do_` call on a genuinely plural match uses "obs"/"lvs". `make_error_message()`'s own OBJ-family branch implemented (`query_the_short()`, an object's own "the_short" apply, falling back to "the thing"). `get_the_error()` generalized into a new `errorInfoToValue()` shared by both its own real call site (`obj == -1`) and `push_bitvec_as_array()`'s own per-rejected-candidate calls with a real object index: every error kind is now reachable and produces the real master `parser_error_message()` call shape (`IsNot`/`NotLiving`/`NotAccessible`'s own 4-arg noun-name-plus-plural-flag form, `Ambig`'s own object array, `Ordinal`'s own count, `ThereIsNo`'s own matched-text, `BadMultiple`'s own bare 2-arg form) except `ManyPaths`, which stays genuinely unreachable (two-object-only). `push_bitvec_as_array()`'s own real, easy-to-miss quirk reproduced exactly: the accepted-objects portion of its array comes out in *descending* object-index order (real code's own "n--" fill loop scanning candidates ascending), confirmed via a dedicated regression test rather than assumed ascending.

**Deliberately scoped to objectTokenCount <= 1, confirmed genuinely separable rather than forced smaller:** a new `VerbRuleNode::objectTokenCount` field (0/1/2) lets `parseRulesFor()` skip any node needing two object tokens, the same honest "not yet supported, not silently wrong" stance already used elsewhere in this row. The real two-object ambiguity family, `dependent_check_functions()`/`check_object_relations()` (packages/parser.c:2184-2493), which decides which direct/indirect object *pair* is jointly valid for a rule like "give OBJ to LIV", is confirmed real, larger, genuinely separate code (a full direct/indirect candidate cross-product walk with its own `CHECK_DIRECT_OK`/`CHECK_INDIRECT_OK`/`CHECK_RELATION_COMPLETE` state machine), not attempted this session, exactly matching the immediately preceding session's own "the natural follow-on after item 8 lands, not before it" prediction. `parse_my_rules()` (the one remaining item-11 efun name, a thin `parse_sentence()` variant) also remains unimplemented, unaffected by this slice.

One real naming-formula bug caught and fixed during this session's own work, before it ever reached a committed test: an initial reading of `make_function()`'s own `omatch+1 >= which` OBS/LVS-naming condition used `omatch`'s *post*-increment value (always 1 for a single-object rule), which would have made the final `do_` call on a plural match always spell "obj" instead of "obs": re-derived directly against source with the real pre-increment ordering, confirmed by a dedicated regression test exercising the plural do_-call naming specifically (`testParseObjAllOfPluralResolvesToTheAcceptedCandidatesOnly`), not merely trusted after the fix.

9 new regression tests (`test/test_lexer.cpp`), each building a real room/player/item environment tree via real `move_object()` calls (the same pattern the immediately preceding slice's own `loadObjects()` tests established): a single `OBJ` rule resolving end to end through the generic `can_`, per-candidate `direct_`, and final `do_` calls, confirming the REAL object identity is received at each stage, not a placeholder; a single candidate rejected by `direct_get_obj()` falling back to the real generic `make_error_message()` text via `master()->parser_error_message()` (`ERR_ALLOCATED` == 6); two indistinguishable candidates producing a real `ERR_AMBIG` (value 4) with `push_undefined()`'s own real int-0 second argument and the two ambiguous objects in real code's own confirmed descending-index array order; an adjective chain narrowing to the correctly-adjectived candidate; an ordinal ("the second sword") resolving to the true Nth accepted candidate, not merely "some" candidate; `LIV_MODIFIER` excluding a non-living object sharing the exact same noun as a living one; "all of" (`OBS`, plural) resolving to an array of exactly the accepted candidates, with a rejected candidate silently excluded rather than aborting the whole match, and the real "obs" (not "obj") do_-call naming confirmed; the fixed "my" adjective resolving to the player's own carried item over an identically-named item lying in the room; and a two-object-token rule confirmed genuinely skipped (no `do_` function ever fires) rather than attempted and silently mismatched. 670 tests passing (up from 661), zero regressions.

Driver process confirmed to stay healthy throughout via a second, independent connection afterward running ordinary gameplay (`eval return 6*7;` -> 42, `who`).) |
| 0.14 | `global include file` config support (auto-`#include` prepended to every compiled object) | `src/config` + `src/object` | [x] |
| 0.15 | `ObjectManager::compile()`'s `programCache_` has no invalidation path: a recompiled file's own new source is silently ignored | `src/object` | [x] |

**0.15 scope note:** found live (2026-08-21) while confirming `mudlib`'s
own `eval` command works end to end. `ObjectManager::compile(filename)`
checks `programCache_` first and returns the cached `CompiledProgram` for
any filename it has ever successfully compiled before, unconditionally,
with no path anywhere that invalidates or replaces that entry: confirmed
directly by reading the function, not inferred from the symptom alone.
Real, reproducible failure mode: `mudlib/command/eval.c` writes a new
`/tmp_eval_file.c`, destructs the previous `/tmp_eval_file` instance, then
calls `"/tmp_eval_file"->eval()` again: the destruct correctly clears
the *instance*, but the *compiled program* for that filename is still
sitting in `programCache_` from the first call, so every subsequent `eval`
silently re-runs the first call's own stale bytecode against the new
instance instead of the newly-written source. Confirmed live: three
different real `eval` expressions in one session (`5+5`, an array
literal, `this_player()`) all returned the first call's own cached result.
Same shape hit live again (2026-08-18) verifying `wand_of_creation.c`'s
own `cmd_create()`, which does the identical rm()+destruct()+write_file()
cycle for a reused item name.

**Fixed 2026-08-18.** `ObjectManager::compile()` now re-reads the target
file's raw (pre-preprocessing) source on every call, before trusting a
`programCache_` hit: the file's current bytes are compared against the
exact source text that produced the cached entry (`programSource_`, a new
parallel map keyed the same way as `programCache_`). Identical content
still takes the fast path (no wasted recompile); a genuine difference
falls through and recompiles fresh, replacing both map entries. Deliberately
a byte comparison, not an mtime check: two writes to the same path within
one filesystem timestamp tick are routine (this row's own regression tests
hit it), and would look unchanged to `stat()` despite genuinely different
content. If the source file has vanished entirely since the cached compile
(a purge with nothing written back), the last good compiled program is
still served rather than treated as an error, matches real semantics,
where an already-compiled program does not stop working just because its
own source file was later deleted. Existing objects already holding the
old `CompiledProgram` (own `shared_ptr`, `LpcObject::program_`) are
unaffected either way: replacing `programCache_`'s own entry never mutates
the old `CompiledProgram` object itself. Scope held to exactly what was
asked: only the recompiled file's own text is checked, a `#include`d
header changing independently, with the including file's own bytes
untouched, is not detected (nothing currently needs it; the demonstrated
failure mode is always the file itself being rewritten). An inheriting
file already compiled against the old version of something it inherits
also keeps that old version until it is itself recompiled (its own
`inheritedPrograms` entry was resolved and copied at *its* own compile
time, not re-resolved retroactively): unchanged from this row's own
prior scoping note, and outside what either real repro (`eval.c`,
`wand_of_creation.c`, both leaf files with no inherits) needed. 4 new
regression tests in `test/test_lexer.cpp`, driving `ObjectManager::
loadObject()`/`cloneObject()` directly (the two real callers of `compile()`)
rather than through either mudlib file: same-path recompile after
destruct+rewrite via `loadObject()`; the same via `cloneObject()` (which
has no `sourceFileExists()` pre-gate, unlike `loadObject()`, so it is the
one that actually reaches `compile()`'s own "source vanished" fallback);
unchanged-source reuse (`programPtr()` identity, confirming the fast path
still avoids wasteful recompiles); and the vanished-source fallback itself
via `cloneObject()`. Verified live against the real running `amlp` binary
(`etc/driver_lil.cfg`), the same way the wand's own live verification was
done: three `eval` calls in one session, all against the same
`/tmp_eval_file` path (`return 5+5`, `return 9999`, `return 5+5` again),
each correctly returning its own fresh result (`10`, `9999`, `10`) instead
of the first call's own stale bytecode.

## Phase 1 - Dialect universality

either, not three-way parity with DGD.** Scope clarified 2026-08-18:
DGD is a comparison-only reference dialect from here on, not a required
target for Phase 1 completion. It stays genuinely useful for that
purpose, `temp/dgd/`'s own real source is still worth reading whenever
a FluffOS/LDMud design question benefits from seeing how a third,
architecturally different driver solved the same problem (real DGD's own
`nil`/planes/LWO model already informed rows 1.10-1.14's own research
below), but no DGD-only row (`1.11`-`1.15`, and any future one) blocks
Phase 1 completion, and none should be read as carrying the same weight
as an actual FluffOS/LDMud gap. The real research already recorded in
each stays on the record; only the priority changes.

(2) DGD's real `atomic` function-declaration modifier (`temp/dgd/src/comp/parser.y`'s own `"ATOMIC { $$ = C_ATOMIC; }"`, confirmed in the scoping note) is now lexed as `TokenType::Keyword` only when `dialect_ == LpcDialect::DGD`; under FluffOS/LDMud it stays a plain `Ident`, matching this file's own "array" precedent for not reserving a word neither real dialect reserves. 2 new regression tests confirm all three dialects directly at the token level. **`nil` implemented 2026-08-18 (continued) as this row's next greenlit slice: see row 1.10 for the full detail (that row now owns the `Value`-level half of this work).** `#'`, `'name`, `rlimits` all remain unimplemented, exactly as scoped, none of them touched this pass. **Mapping width investigated 2026-08-18 (continued) and confirmed bigger than this row's own "moderate" classification, not implemented, see row 1.9 for the full finding; it now owns this literal syntax too, not split across two rows.** **`#'name` bare-name closure literal first slice implemented 2026-08-18 (continued, real Phase 1 commitment, not scoping-only): picked as the highest real-world-compatibility item among Phase 1's three remaining actual blockers (`#'`/`'name` closures, mapping width, connect/disconnect), see this session's own STATUS.md entry for the full assessment and reasoning. `#` is not lexable at all today outside real preprocessor directives (confirmed: falls straight to `tokenize()`'s own "unrecognized character" branch, not merely misread as an `Ident` the way `atomic`/`nil` were), new `Lexer::lexHashQuote()`, dispatched only when `dialect_ == LpcDialect::LdMud && peekNext() == '\''`, combines `#` + `'` into one two-character `Symbol` token (`"#'"`), matching the existing `"->"`/`"::"`/`"++"` multi-char-symbol precedent rather than a new `TokenType`. Deliberately the bare-name form only, no operator spellings (`#'+`, `#'==`, ...), no `#'[` index/range forms, no `#'({` aggregate, no `#'efun::`/`#'sefun::`/`#'lfun::`/`#'var::`/`#'Name::` scope prefixes, all of which stay exactly as unimplemented as before. Also fixed the one genuinely architecture-touching prerequisite the row 1.2/1.3 scoping note below had already identified but left unsolved: real system `cpp` hard-errors on any line whose first non-whitespace character is `#` and is not a real directive it recognizes, which a bare `"#'this_player;"` statement on its own line, a completely ordinary way to write it, would trigger, taking down the *whole file's* preprocessing regardless of what the Lexer/Parser are taught to recognize. Fixed in `ObjectManager.cpp` (`maskHashQuote()`/`unmaskHashQuote()`): every `#'` occurrence in the raw source is masked to a plain-identifier marker text before staging for `cpp`, then unmasked back in `runPreprocessor()`'s own output, runs unconditionally for every file regardless of dialect (nothing dialect-specific about the fix itself), so a file with no `#'` anywhere is untouched either way. See row 1.3's own entry for the Parser-side half and why it needed zero new CodeGen/VM work.**) |
