| 2.56 | `func_spec.c` `USE_ICONV` conversion pair: `str_to_arr(string)`, `arr_to_str(int *)` | `src/efun` | [x] (added 2026-08-30, a clean self-contained slice: a UTF-8 string decoded to an array of code points and back, no LPC re-entry, no new subsystem, no dependency, no buffer type. **2.9-only surface:** `temp/reference/fluffos-2.9-ds2.08/func_spec.c:398-399` declares `int *str_to_arr(string); string arr_to_str(int *);` inside `#ifdef USE_ICONV`, bodied in `fliconv.c` (`f_str_to_arr()` at :159, `f_arr_to_str()` at :178). The current locally-vendored clone `temp/fluffos/src/` dropped both when FluffOS moved to always-on Unicode (`ChangeLog.fluffos-2.x`: "added str_to_arr, and arr_to_str efuns to convert between strings and UTF-32 arrays" is the last mention), so 2.9 ds2.08 is the sole reference, the same single-tree basis `string_difference()` (row 2.52) noted. Grep of `EfunTable.cpp` confirmed neither was registered; `strwidth`, the third name in the same `USE_ICONV` block, is left as its already-registered non-`USE_ICONV` `sizeof` alias (a pre-existing choice with its own row, not revisited). **Semantics** traced from `fliconv.c`: `str_to_arr(s)` iconv-converts `s` from UTF-8 to UTF-32 over `SVALUE_STRLEN(sp)+1` bytes (i.e. including the terminating NUL) then returns an int array of the resulting 32-bit units (`len /= 4`), so the returned array always carries a trailing `0` element (`str_to_arr("AB")` -> `({ 65, 66, 0 })`, `str_to_arr("")` -> `({ 0 })`, an embedded NUL byte becomes a `0` element in place); `arr_to_str(a)` treats each element as a UTF-32 code point, appends a `0`, iconv-converts UTF-32 -> UTF-8, and returns the result via `copy_and_push_string()` (which stops at the first NUL), so `arr_to_str(({ 65, 66 }))` -> `"AB"`, an embedded `0` truncates (`arr_to_str(({ 65, 0, 66 }))` -> `"A"`), `arr_to_str(({}))` -> `""`. The two are inverses for valid text: `arr_to_str(str_to_arr(s)) == s`, the trailing `0` `str_to_arr` appends being exactly the NUL `arr_to_str` stops on. **Named local choices, none a silent divergence:** (1) implemented as a direct UTF-8 <-> code-point codec rather than through a live iconv dependency; iconv `"UTF-8"` <-> `"UTF-32"` is exactly a code-point transcode, real's translator name is `"UTF-32//TRANSLIT//IGNORE"` on Linux and `str_to_arr`'s one-time warm-up call (`translate_easy(newt->outgoing, " ")`) exists only to consume iconv's leading UTF-32 BOM, so the observable LPC-level values are BOM-free code points, which is what this codec produces. (2) Malformed input is ignored, matching real's `//IGNORE`: an invalid UTF-8 sequence (bad lead byte, truncated or bad continuation, overlong, surrogate, or `> U+10FFFF`) is skipped by `str_to_arr`; an out-of-range, surrogate, or negative code point is skipped by `arr_to_str`. A float array element is truncated to an integer code point; any other non-integer element throws. (3) `max_string_length` is not enforced (this driver has none, the same situation `add_a()` / `replace_html()` are in). A non-string argument to `str_to_arr`, or a non-array argument to `arr_to_str`, throws `LpcRuntimeError` (this codebase's established bad-shape precedent). **Corpus call-site frequency, checked before implementing:** grepped every vendored corpus under `temp/` (`core-lib`, `dead-souls`, `es2_mudlib`, `lima`, `nightmare3`, `reference-lpc-mud-library`, `wiz_tools`, `lil`) plus the bundled `mudlib/` for `str_to_arr(` / `arr_to_str(`: zero real LPC call sites. Motivation is FluffOS-surface parity, the same honestly-named basis as rows 2.46/2.50/2.51/2.52/2.53/2.54/2.55; UTF-8 round-tripping is independently verifiable by hand against known code points with no live-instance dependency. **1 new regression test (813 total, up from 812):** `testStrToArrAndArrToStrRoundTripUtf8` -- `str_to_arr` on ASCII, the empty string, a 2-byte (`U+00E9`), a 3-byte (`U+20AC`) and a 4-byte (`U+1F600`) character each decode to one code point plus the trailing `0`; an embedded NUL byte yields a `0` element in place; a lone `0xFF`, a truncated 2-byte lead, and a surrogate sequence (`0xED 0xA0 0x80`) all drop out leaving just the terminator; `arr_to_str` performs the inverse encodings, truncates at an embedded `0`, and skips an out-of-range (`0x110000`) or surrogate (`0xD800`) code point; `arr_to_str(str_to_arr(s)) == s` for three mixed ASCII/multibyte strings; and a non-string to `str_to_arr`, a non-array to `arr_to_str`, and a non-integer array element reached before any `0` all throw. Every expected value is a hand-computed UTF-8 encoding of a known code point, not read back from this driver. See `STATUS.md`'s own dated entry for the full trail.) |
| 2.57 | `replace_string()` occurrence-range form: optional 4th `first` / 5th `last` occurrence-index arguments | `src/efun` | [x] (added 2026-08-30, an extension of an already-registered efun rather than a new name: pure string work, no new subsystem, no dependency, no buffer type, no LPC re-entry. **Real surface, present in both trees:** `temp/reference/fluffos-2.9-ds2.08/func_spec.c:118` declares `string replace_string(string, string, string,...);` (varargs), `efun_defs.c:195` gives min-args 3, max-args -1; the current locally-vendored clone `temp/fluffos/src/` carries the identical varargs signature. This driver's `replace_string` was registered with the 3-argument body only and threw `LpcRuntimeError` ("occurrence-range form not implemented") on any 4th/5th argument. **Semantics** traced line-by-line from `efuns_main.c`'s `f_replace_string()` (:2326-2560, John Garnett's skip-table version): more than 5 arguments errors "Too many args to replace_string."; `first`/`last` both default to 0; with 4 arguments the 4th is `last` and `first` stays 0 (so occurrences 1..last are replaced), with 5 arguments the 4th is `first` and the 5th is `last`; each occurrence bound must be an int (`CHECK_TYPES(..., T_NUMBER, ...)`, so a float throws); `last == 0` means "no upper bound" (real `if (!last) last = max_string_length;`), which for this driver, having no `max_string_length` at all (the same situation `add_a()` / `replace_html()` / `str_to_arr()` are in), is simply unbounded; `if (first > last)` (evaluated after that default, so an unbounded upper bound never trips it) returns the string unchanged; an empty pattern returns the string unchanged (`if (!plen) ... just return it`); matches are counted whether or not they fall in `[first, last]`, an out-of-range match copied through verbatim; and the scan stops the instant the `last`-th match is handled (`if (cur == last) break;`), the remainder copied unchanged. A negative bound is left as-is: it either trips the `first > last` early return or makes the `cur <= last` test never true, matching real, which likewise does not clamp. The real `max_string_length` overflow path (a `const0u`, i.e. T_UNDEFINED 0, return) has no analogue here, this driver having no such limit, a named local choice of the same kind rows 2.55/2.56 recorded. **Corpus call-site frequency, checked before implementing:** unlike the recent parity-only rows, the occurrence-limit form has strong, widespread real corpus evidence. Grepping every vendored corpus under `temp/` (`core-lib`, `dead-souls`, `es2_mudlib`, `lima`, `nightmare3`, `reference-lpc-mud-library`, `wiz_tools`, `lil`) plus the bundled `mudlib/` for `replace_string(...,...,...,` turned up many real 4-argument "replace only the first occurrence" call sites, e.g. `replace_string(line, "Chapter", "chapter", 1)`, `replace_string(skill, "in ", "", 1)`, `replace_string(key, "Get", "Set", 1)`, `replace_string(stamp, ".", "/", 1)`, `replace_string(mess, "<" + chan + ">", colorchan, 1)`, plus doc pages carrying the 5-argument form's own worked vectors. Every one of those previously threw against this driver. **1 new regression test (814 total, up from 813):** `testReplaceStringOccurrenceRangeForm` -- the 3-argument form still replaces every occurrence; FluffOS's own documented vectors `replace_string("xyxx", "x", "z", 2)` -> `"zyzx"` and `replace_string("xyxxy", "x", "z", 2, 3)` -> `"xyzzy"`; the corpus-common `replace_string("Chapter 1, Chapter 2", "Chapter", "chapter", 1)` -> `"chapter 1, Chapter 2"`; a `last` past the match count and a `last` of 0 both replace all; a 5-argument `first` with `last` 0 replaces from the Nth occurrence onward; `first > last` returns the string unchanged; a replacement longer or shorter than the pattern still tracks the occurrence count rather than byte offsets; an empty pattern is a no-op even with a range; and a non-int bound (a float through a `mixed`) and a 6-argument call both throw. Expected outputs traced by hand from `f_replace_string()`'s own counting rule, not read back from this driver. See `STATUS.md`'s own dated entry for the full trail.) |
| 2.58 | `strsrch()` / `strstr()`: int-char needle, and 3rd argument is a direction flag not a start index | `src/efun` | [x] (added 2026-08-30, a correctness fix of an already-registered efun, found by a targeted corpus pass for other efuns of the same class as row 2.57's `replace_string` gap: called with an argument count or type the current body rejects or misreads. `strsrch` was the highest-frequency such case (76 distinct affected call-site lines across seven corpora, versus ~49 for the next, `get_dir`'s stat-flag form, which is left as a follow-up). **The bug had two parts, one a silent wrong answer:** (1) the 2nd argument, real `string | int` (`func_spec.c:125` `int strsrch(string, string | int, int default: 0);`, `efun_defs.c:251` arg types `T_STRING, T_STRING|T_NUMBER, T_NUMBER`), was required to be a string, so the very common `strsrch(flags, '1')` int-char-needle idiom threw `LpcRuntimeError`; (2) the 3rd argument was implemented as a MudOS-style start index ("first index of needle at or after start"), but real `f_strsrch()` (`efuns_main.c:3059`, Luke Mewburn 930706) treats it as a DIRECTION FLAG: `!((sp+1)->u.number)` searches left to right (`strchr`/`strstr`, first match), any non-zero value searches right to left (`strrchr` / a hand-rolled reverse substring scan, last match). So `strsrch(path, "/", -1)`, the standard "offset of the final slash" path-splitting idiom, was silently returning -1 (the old code saw `start = -1 < 0` and bailed) instead of the last slash's index. **Real semantics, ported from `f_strsrch()`:** an int needle is `buf[0] = (char) sp->u.number`, i.e. the low 8 bits, and a resulting NUL byte (int 0, or any multiple of 256) is an empty needle; an empty needle or a needle longer than the haystack returns -1 (`if (!llen || blen < llen) pos = NULL`); the result is the byte offset or -1. `strstr` is a real alias (`func_spec.c:127` `int strstr strsrch(...);`) and shares the body. **Named local choice:** real's `strchr`/`strstr`/`strrchr` stop at an embedded NUL, so the haystack and a string needle are taken up to their first NUL, the same choice rows 2.52/2.55 recorded. **Corpus evidence:** 51 call-site lines use an int-char needle (`strsrch(flags, '1')`, `strsrch(flags, 'l')`, `strsrch(flags, 'C')`, `strsrch(prop, '/', -1)`, ...), 33 pass a 3rd argument (almost all the literal `-1` backward-search idiom: `strsrch(path, "/", -1)`, `strsrch(remains, "@", -1)`, `strsrch(prop, '/', -1)` x4, ...); union 76 distinct lines, every one previously either throwing or returning a wrong index. **1 new regression test (815 total, up from 814):** `testStrsrchIntNeedleAndBackwardFlag` -- forward vs backward for string and int-char needles; the `strsrch(s, '/', -1)` last-slash idiom; a NUL int needle (0 and 256) and an out-of-8-bit-range needle (`'A' + 256` still matches `'A'`); empty-needle, needle-longer-than-haystack, and not-found all -1; `strstr` taking the same flag; and a non-string/non-int needle, a non-int flag, and a 4-argument call all throwing. The pre-existing `testStrlenAndStrstrAliasesWorkByTheirOwnNames` had encoded the old start-index reading (`strstr("aXaXa", "a", 1)` expecting 2); it now expects 4, the rightmost `a`, with its comment corrected. Expected offsets traced by hand from `f_strsrch()`, not read back from this driver. See `STATUS.md`'s own dated entry for the full trail.) |
| 2.59 | `get_dir(string, int flags)`: the optional stat-flag argument (`-1` returns `({ name, size, mtime })` triples) | `src/efun` | [x] (added 2026-08-30, the follow-up row 2.58's own STATUS entry named as the next-highest-frequency case of an already-registered efun rejecting a real argument shape: `get_dir` threw `LpcRuntimeError("get_dir: flags argument not implemented")` on any non-zero 2nd argument, and roughly 49 distinct real call-site lines across the vendored corpora pass one (26 pass `-1`, 19 pass `0x10`, plus a few `0x07`/`0x17`). **Real surface:** `func_spec.c:124` `mixed *get_dir(string, int default: 0);`, `efun_defs.c:94` (2 args, 2nd `T_NUMBER`). **Semantics traced from `file.c` `get_dir()` + `encode_stat()` (identical in the pinned 2.9 tree and the current clone `temp/fluffos/src/packages/core/file.cc`):** `encode_stat()` special-cases only `flags == -1`, emitting a 3-element array `({ name, size, mtime })` where `size` is `-2` for a directory and `st_size` for a regular file (`(st->st_mode & S_IFDIR) ? -2 : st->st_size`) and `mtime` is `st_mtime`; every other `flags` value (`0`, and the `0x10` newer mudlibs pass) falls to the plain-name branch, so a non-`-1` flag is accepted and behaves exactly like `0`, not an error. The result is sorted by name in both forms (real `qsort` with `pstrcmp` / `parrcmp`); this driver's `get_dir` previously returned entries in raw `readdir` order, so sorting is now applied to the no-flag path too, a more-correct change the one pre-existing `get_dir` test does not depend on (it sorts names itself before asserting). A path resolving to a single existing file yields a 1-element array; an invalid path returns an empty result (this driver returns an empty array where real returns int `0` for a missing parent directory, a pre-existing minor divergence left out of scope). The glob-in-final-component matching, `.`/`..` exclusion, and path gating are unchanged. **1 new regression test (817 total, up from 816):** `testGetDirFlagsFormReturnsStatTriplesOrNames` -- `get_dir("/", -1)` returns `({ name, size, mtime })` triples with a 5-byte file reporting `5`, a 3-byte file `3`, and a subdirectory `-2`, a plausible recent `mtime`, and the entries sorted by name; `get_dir("/*.txt", -1)` is the glob plus the triple shape; `get_dir("/alpha.txt", -1)` is a 1-element array holding one triple; `get_dir("/", 16)` (i.e. `0x10`) returns the same plain name strings as `get_dir("/")`; a missing path yields nothing without throwing; and a non-int flags argument throws. Expected sizes are the literal byte lengths of the test files, not read back from this driver. See `STATUS.md`'s own dated entry for the full trail.) |
| 2.60 | `sprintf` `%=` column / word-wrap mode (single-column form) | `src/efun` | [x] (added 2026-08-30. Row 3.9's own STATUS notes flagged this as a "confirmed real need" reachable in ordinary play (`cmds/mortal/_emote.c`'s `cmd_emote()` throws `sprintf: unsupported format specifier '%='`); it is also one of the most widespread sprintf modifiers across the vendored corpora (40+ files, ~30 distinct call-site lines: `core-lib`, `dead-souls`, `lima`, `nightmare3`, `reference-lpc-mud-library`, `wiz_tools`), overwhelmingly the `sprintf("%-=" + WIDTH + "s", str)` "wrap a message to the terminal width" idiom. **Semantics traced from `temp/reference/fluffos-2.9-ds2.08/sprintf.c` (`INFO_COLS`, `add_column()`, `add_justified()`):** `%=Ns` (or `%=NO`) word-wraps its string argument to a column N wide, greedy word break (back up to the last space when a fill would split a word), an embedded `\n` forcing a break, wrapped lines joined by `\n`. The wrap width is the precision if one is given, clamped to the field size, else the field size (`if (pres > fs) pres = fs; (pres) ? pres : fs`); a field size is required (`if (!fs) ERROR(ERR_CST_REQUIRES_FS)`). `-` left-justifies each wrapped line, `|` centres it (odd pad character on the leading side), neither right-justifies; trailing pad on a wrapped line is added only when more non-`\n` format text follows the specifier (real's `trailing` flag). Continuation lines are indented to the output column where `%=` began, so `%s%-=*s` over a prefix plus body produces a real hanging indent. `%=` on a numeric specifier (`%=5d`) is a silent no-op, exactly as real (its INT branch never enters `INFO_COLS`), so the existing field-width path handles it. **Scoped out, named:** the interleaved MULTI-column form (two or more `%=` string specifiers in one format, the wizard file-listing table `sprintf("%=9s %=8s %=7s ...", ...)`) needs the row-at-a-time rebuild real `string_print()` does and throws a clear `multi-column '%=' layout is not implemented` error rather than mislaying the columns; `#`/`@`/`'X'`/` `/`+`/`%f`/`%X` stay unimplemented as before. **1 new regression test (818 total, up from 817):** `testSprintfColumnModeWordWrap` -- `%-=10s` over a 25-char sentence wraps to `"the quick\nbrown fox\njumps"`; a trailing `\n` in the format is appended as normal; default (no `-`) right-justifies each line (`"%=10s"` over `"ab cd ef"` gives `"  ab cd ef"`); `|` centres (`"%=|8s"` over `"hi"` gives `"   hi"`); an embedded `\n` forces a break; a `%s` prefix hang-indents the continuation lines (`"NOTE: aaa bbb\n      ccc ddd"`); `*` dynamic width feeds the wrap width; `%=5d)` formats as `"   42)"`; a short string needs no wrap; and two `%=` string specifiers, or a `%=` with no field width, both throw. Every expected string was traced by hand from `add_column()` / `add_justified()`, not read back from this driver. See `STATUS.md`'s own dated entry for the full trail.) |
| 2.61 | `implode()` function/fold form, and non-string skipping in the join form | `src/efun` | [x] (added 2026-09-01, the same one-package-at-a-time discipline rows 2.57-2.60 used: extend an already-registered efun to the real argument shapes its current body rejects, rather than registering a new name. `implode`'s own code comment already flagged the function form as "not implemented", and row 2.58's own STATUS entry named `implode(arr, function [, seed])` (the reduce form) as a known remaining gap, "lower frequency than the two above and needs a closure trampoline. Noted, not scheduled here." **Real surface:** `temp/reference/fluffos-2.9-ds2.08/func_spec.c:76` `mixed implode(mixed *, string | function, void | mixed);`, `efun_defs.c:99` (2..3 args, 2nd arg `T_STRING|T_FUNCTION`, 3rd `T_ANY`); identical in the current clone `temp/fluffos/src/packages/core/core.spec:55`. **Real semantics, read line by line from `temp/reference/fluffos-2.9-ds2.08/efuns_main.c:1032` `f_implode()` plus `array.c:395` `implode_string()` and `array.c:428` `implode_array()`:** (1) 2nd arg a string is the join form, and real `implode_string()` counts only `T_STRING` elements, puts the separator between consecutive string elements only, skips non-string elements entirely (not stringified, not an error), and returns `""` when no element is a string; a 3rd argument here is a hard error (`"Third argument to implode() is illegal with implode(array, string)"`). (2) 2nd arg a function is a left fold calling the function with exactly two arguments `(accumulator, element)` each step: with a seed (3 args) `acc = seed` then `acc = f(acc, elem)` for every element, an empty array returning the seed unchanged; without a seed (2 args) an empty array returns int `0`, a one-element array returns that element unchanged with the function never called, otherwise `acc = arr[0]` then `acc = f(acc, arr[i])` for `i` in `1..n-1`. **Corpus evidence:** most of the ~1200 `implode(` call sites across the vendored corpora pass a literal string separator (already handled). The function form's own conformance case is pinned directly by `temp/lil/single/tests/efuns/implode.c`'s own 9 assertions (`implode(({1,2,3}),(: $1 + $2 :)) == 6`, `implode(({1,2,3}),(: $1 + $2 :),"") == "123"`, `implode(({}),(: $1 :),666) == 666`, `implode(({}),(: $1 :)) == 0`, `implode(({555}),(: $1 + 5 :)) == 555`, plus the join-form non-string-skipping cases `implode(({1,2,3}),"foo") == ""` and `implode(({1,"foo","bar"}),"bazz") == "foobazzbar"`), and `temp/dead-souls/lib/doc/efun/strings/implode` documents the same. **Built:** the `implode` registration in `EfunTable.cpp` rewritten to dispatch on the 2nd argument (`std::string` -> join, `std::shared_ptr<Closure>` -> fold via `vm.callClosure(closure, {acc, elem})`, the same mechanism `map`/`filter`/`sort_array` already use), 2 or 3 args only, a non-string/non-function 2nd arg or a 4th arg throwing. The prior body's "throw on a non-string element" was itself a divergence from real `implode_string()` and is now the real skip behavior; strictly more lenient, so no existing all-string call site changes. **Scoped out, named:** `check_for_destr(arr)` (real drops destructed object refs to `0` before folding) is not reproduced (no corpus call site depends on it, and the prior body did not do it either); real's `if (!v) *dest = const0` mid-fold recovery for a call through a destructed-object function pointer is approximated by a null-`Closure` guard returning `0`, since `vm.callClosure()` throws rather than returning a sentinel, the same choice `map`/`filter` make. **1 new regression test (819 total, up from 818):** `testImplodeFunctionFoldFormAndNonStringSkipping` in `test/test_lexer.cpp`, covering every `temp/lil` assertion plus the string-separator-with-3rd-argument error path. Every expected value traced by hand from `f_implode()`/`implode_string()`/`implode_array()`, not read back from this driver. The existing 818 re-run unchanged. See `STATUS.md`'s own dated entry for the full trail. `COMPARISON.md` had a full phase-fraction re-derivation earlier this same session (Phase 2 33/61); its numbers were nudged by one for this row (Phase 2 34/62, rollups 61/98 and 61/103) so the two documents stay consistent, rather than left one row apart.) |
| 2.62 | `sprintf` `%f` float specifier, `%i` alias, and `+`/space pad-prefix flags | `src/efun` | [x] (added 2026-09-01, continuing the same one-package-at-a-time discipline of rows 2.57-2.61: extend an already-registered efun to the real argument shapes its body rejects. `sprintf`'s own top comment already listed `%f` (float), `%X`, and the `" "`/`"+"` pad-prefix flags among the real modifiers it does not implement; a `%f` call threw `sprintf: unsupported format specifier '%f'`. **Real surface:** `temp/reference/fluffos-2.9-ds2.08/sprintf.c:911` `case 'f': finfo |= INFO_T_FLOAT;` and `:906-909` `case 'd': case 'i': finfo |= INFO_T_INT;` (so `i` is a plain alias of `d`), plus `:869-873` `case ' ': finfo |= INFO_PP_SPACE;` / `case '+': finfo |= INFO_PP_PLUS;`. **Real semantics, read from `sprintf.c:1134-1211` (the shared numeric branch) plus `add_justified()` at `:562`:** a C cheat string is built as `"%"` + (`"+"` for `INFO_PP_PLUS`, `" "` for `INFO_PP_SPACE`) + (`".<pres>"` only when `if (pres)` is true, i.e. precision is nonzero) + the type char (`f` for float, `ld` for int), then run through C `sprintf` on `carg->u.real` (float) or `carg->u.number` (int); the field width `fs` is NOT baked into the cheat string, it is applied afterward by `add_justified()` (pad to `fs`, `-` left / `|` centre / default right, `0`-prefix pads with `'0'` ahead of any sign, exactly as this driver's existing `%d` field-width block already does). The float argument must be a real: `sprintf.c:1180` errors `"Incorrect argument type to %f"` when `cheat` ends in `f` and `carg->type != T_REAL`, so an int is not silently coerced. Precision 0 (a bare `%f`, or an explicit `%.0f`) means C's default six places, per real's own `if (pres)` guard that only appends `.pres` for a nonzero precision, a deliberate real quirk reproduced here. **Corpus evidence:** `%f`-family specifiers appear in about 20 distinct files across `core-lib`, `dead-souls`, `es2_mudlib`, `lima`, `nightmare3`, `reference-lpc-mud-library`, and `wiz_tools`, roughly 60 call-site lines, dominated by `%.2f`/`%9.2f`/`%3.1f`/`%2.2f` for weights, money, CPU-time, and percentages (e.g. a weight-to-string simul_efun's `sprintf("%.2f lbs", w)`), plus `%+4.2f` (signed) and a scattering of `%.3f`/`%16.4f`/`%10.4f`. `%i`/`%3i` appears in FTP-daemon and status-report code across several corpora; `%+d` in roughly 8 call-site lines (signed stat deltas). Every one of these previously threw. **Built** (`EfunTable.cpp`, `sprintfImpl`): the modifier scan now also consumes `+` and ` ` (a lone one directly after `%` or another modifier, so ordinary literal spaces between specifiers are untouched, covered by a regression test); `spec == 'i'` joins the `spec == 'd'` branch; `%d`/`%i` with a sign flag format through C `"%+lld"`/`"% lld"` instead of `std::to_string`; a new `spec == 'f'` branch requires a `double` value (throws `sprintf: %f argument is not a float` otherwise, matching real), builds the `"%[+ ][.pres]f"` cheat string, sizes the output with a zero-length `std::snprintf` probe then formats into the `piece` string, and lets the existing shared field-width/justify/zero-pad block finish it. **Scoped out, named:** `%X` (capital hex), `#` (table mode), `@` (array-spread), `'X'` (custom pad string), and the `:` (colon) precision-tie combined with `%f` are still not implemented (zero corpus evidence, `%f` with `:` never appears); `+`/` ` on `%o`/`%x` is accepted by the modifier scan but has no effect, matching C where those flags are meaningless for unsigned conversions. **1 new regression test (820 total, up from 819):** `testSprintfFloatSpecifierAndSignFlags` in `test/test_lexer.cpp`, covering the six-place default, explicit precision, the `%.0f`-is-six-places quirk, field width right/left/zero-pad, `+` and ` ` sign flags on both signs, the int-argument-to-`%f` throw, `%i`/`%3i`, `%+d`/`% d`, and a literal space between two specifiers staying literal. Every expected string traced by hand from `sprintf.c`'s cheat-string build and `add_justified()`, not read back from this driver. The existing 819 re-run unchanged. See `STATUS.md`'s own dated entry for the full trail. `COMPARISON.md` had its full 2026-09-01 phase-fraction re-derivation earlier this same session; its numbers were nudged by one for this row too (Phase 2 35/63, rollups 62/99 excluding the 5 DGD-only Phase 1 rows and 62/104 including them, suite 820) so the two documents stay consistent rather than drift a row apart.) |

Real dependency: this row's file-access half consumes row 3.1's `euid()` (real `valid_read`/`valid_write` are handed the calling object's effective uid, and the mudlib's own master maps that uid to a writable path prefix, e.g. `secure/daemon/master.c`'s own `valid_write()`); this driver's `valid_read`/`valid_write` (`EfunTable.cpp`) passed `caller->privs()` as a stand-in for that uid until row 3.1's slice 2 (2026-09-01). Three-part slice: (1) `valid_read`/`valid_write` pass `caller->euid()` instead of `caller->privs()` when the uid model is active -- **done in row 3.1's slice 2, 2026-09-01** (`checkValidPath()`'s LDMud branch, cited to `temp/ldmud/src/simulate.c:3778-3795`; `privs()` kept only as the inactive-model fallback); (2) the `clone_object()` euid guard (`simulate.c:545-549`) -- **also done in row 3.1's slice 2** (top of `ObjectManager::cloneObject()`, gated on `uidModel_.active()`); (3) re-scoped 2026-09-01 (row 3.1 slice 3 session) to "the `legal_path()` `..`/`#`-character check, which `checkValidPath()`'s own comment already flags as deliberately not replicated" -- **done 2026-09-02.** Real source, read directly: FluffOS 2.9 `temp/reference/fluffos-2.9-ds2.08/file.c:295-334` (`legal_path()`: rejects a leading `/`, any `#` anywhere -- own comment "disallowing # seems the easiest way to solve a bug involving loading files containing that character", real FluffOS filenames use a trailing `#<clone-number>` suffix for cloned-object identity -- and, via its own real pointer-walk, any `..` or bare `.` path component including a *leading* `./`) and `file.c:705-750` (`check_valid_path()`: applies `valid_write`/`valid_read`, honours a `0` return as deny and a string return as a path rewrite, strips one leading `/`, turns an all-slash path into `.`, THEN runs `legal_path()` as the final gate -- confirming there is no second per-domain layer in the driver, the domain-to-path-prefix mapping lives entirely in the mudlib master's own `valid_write()` body). LDMud's equivalent, also read directly: `temp/ldmud/src/simulate.c:1734-1776` (`check_no_parentdirs()` + `legal_path()`: rejects a leading `/`, an embedded space unless `allow_filename_spaces` -- `main.c:139`, default `MY_FALSE`, command-line-only, no equivalent config key in this driver -- and an anchored `..` path component; genuinely narrower than FluffOS's own version, no `#` check at all, and does not reject a bare `./`) and `simulate.c:3752-3851` (`check_valid_path()`: same apply-then-strip-then-`legal_path()` shape as FluffOS, but an illegal path throws a catchable runtime error, `errorf("Illegal path '%s' for %s() by %s\n", ...)` at `:3846-3849`, rather than FluffOS's silent `return 0`). Real corpus evidence this matters: this driver's own `resolveMudlibPath()` (`VM.cpp`) is a bare `mudlibRoot + lpcPath` string concatenation with no sandboxing of its own, and every vendored mudlib corpus builds file-efun paths from LPC string concatenation, much of it player-influenced (login/account-creation flows composing save paths from a player-chosen name being the representative real shape) -- before this slice, a crafted `../../../etc/passwd`-shaped path reaching any of this driver's already-gated file efuns would walk straight past `mudlibRoot` on real disk I/O once the (permissive-by-default) master-apply step passed it through. **Built:** `legalPathFluffos()` and `legalPathLdmud()` (`EfunTable.cpp`, anonymous namespace, right above `checkValidPath()`), each a bounds-checked, line-by-line port of its real body (not a from-scratch "block dot-dot" reimplementation, preserving real quirks like FluffOS's own leading-`./`-is-illegal behavior and LDMud's own leave-embedded-`/./`-alone behavior); `checkValidPath()` now runs the dialect-appropriate one, after the master-apply gate, on the approved path with one leading `/` stripped for the check only (the returned path itself keeps its leading `/`, this driver's own established `resolveMudlibPath()` convention, left unchanged), denying silently under FluffOS and throwing `LpcRuntimeError` with the real `errorf()`-format message under LDMud. **Named divergence:** none beyond the dialects' own real divergence from each other (ported faithfully, not harmonized); a null caller (a shape real LDMud's own `fatal("Illegal caller for check_valid_path.\n")` guard would not permit, but this driver already reaches this point for calls with no current object elsewhere) substitutes `"?"` for the caller name in the LDMud error message rather than crashing. **6 new regression tests (833 total, up from 827):** `testCheckValidPathRejectsParentDirectoryTraversalFluffos`, `testCheckValidPathRejectsEmbeddedHashFluffos`, `testCheckValidPathRejectsLeadingDotSlashFluffos`, `testCheckValidPathAcceptsOrdinaryDottedFilenameFluffos` (the false-positive check -- ordinary dotted filenames still round-trip), `testCheckValidPathLdmudThrowsCatchableRuntimeErrorOnTraversal` (verified through LPC-level `catch()`, not just a native try/catch, proving it is a genuine catchable LPC error matching the real `errorf()` message shape), `testCheckValidPathLdmudRejectsSpaceButAllowsHash` (the two dialects' own genuine, opposite-direction divergence, both directions exercised in one test). Live re-boot of the bundled `mudlib/` (`./build/amlp etc/driver.cfg`, 5 seconds) stayed silent and running the whole time, no compile errors, no crash. **`call_other` capability grants, explicitly out of the real-source-porting scope this row closes:** an object may only `call_other()` into another object's functions it has been granted access to has no real FluffOS analog in the vendored 2.9 source (confirmed again this session, nothing new since the 2026-09-01 finding) -- it is a DGD-style capability model, a genuinely novel design item, not a port, and stays deliberately deferred until and unless it is explicitly wanted; it does not block marking this row `[x]` since it was never part of what this row was scoped to port. **This is also, as of 2026-09-02, the end of the real driver-level uid/security cluster (rows 3.1 + 3.2): every item either row named with an actual real source citation has now been read directly and either built or explicitly ruled out (uid/gid group membership, `call_other` capability grants) as having no real driver-level source to port.** The next real target in this area, if wanted, is genuinely novel design work (`call_other` capability grants above, or a from-scratch per-domain filesystem jail beyond what real `valid_write()`-in-the-mudlib already provides), not a further FluffOS/LDMud port -- recommend picking up a different roadmap row (e.g. 3.3's GC migration, or 3.4's telnet option negotiation) rather than continuing to search this cluster for more real source to port.) |

That gap matters concretely here, more than for most other rows: grepped every real `shared_ptr<LpcObject>`/`shared_ptr<Array>`/`shared_ptr<Mapping>`/`shared_ptr<Closure>` occurrence across `src/`+`include/` directly, 525 real hits today -- the instruct.md's own 5-layer migration plan (no-op wrapper, then arrays, then mappings/closures, then `LpcObject` itself, then generational separation) was scoped against a codebase roughly half this size, so the real surface it touches, especially layer 4's own `LpcObject` migration, explicitly touching `ObjectManager`'s cache, `InteractiveRegistry`, `LivingNameRegistry`, `Scheduler`, and `Server` all at once per the instruct.md's own text, is almost certainly larger in real scope today than when it was written. | 3.4 | Full telnet option negotiation + GMCP, MSDP, MSSP, MTTS, MXP | `src/proto` + `src/net` | [ ] (partial: TELOPT_TTYPE negotiation, real FluffOS 2.9's own complete driver-level MTTS mechanism, built 2026-09-02 -- see below. Scoped by comparing this row against row 3.3 (generational GC) as the two remaining Phase 3 candidates named 2026-09-02: row 3.3's own `src/gc/instruct.md` premise ("replace the shared_ptr-everywhere model with a generational GC") does not match either real driver's actual architecture -- confirmed by reading real source directly rather than trusting the instruct.md: real FluffOS 2.9 is plain reference-counted (`object.h`'s own `ob->ref`/`array.h`'s own `array->ref`, exactly the strategy `shared_ptr` already gives this driver), and its `reclaim.c` (the only file in the vendored tree matching a "gc"-shaped grep) is not a garbage collector at all, just a periodic destructed-object svalue scrubber (`check_svalue()`/`gc_mapping()`, nulling out lingering `T_OBJECT` references to already-destructed objects). Real LDMud (`temp/ldmud/src/gcollect.c`) does have a genuine mark-and-sweep collector, but it is a diagnostic/leak-recovery pass layered on top of still-refcounted memory (triggered by an explicit `garbage_collection()` efun or a memory-pressure threshold), not a continuously-running generational collector replacing refcounting -- row 3.3's own real first slice, if picked up, is therefore porting that leak-detection mechanism onto the existing `shared_ptr` model, not the large from-scratch generational rewrite `src/gc/instruct.md` describes; that rescoping is its own real investigation, not done here. Row 3.4's own real source, by contrast, is small, precisely cited, self-contained (touches only `src/net`, no VM/memory-model risk), and was already flagged as a known gap by this driver's own existing code (`Server.cpp`'s prior comment: "real new_user() also sends IAC DO TTYPE and IAC DO MXP here, neither of which anything in this driver processes yet") -- the smaller, safer, more independently-shippable slice, so it was picked over row 3.3. Real source, read directly: FluffOS 2.9 `temp/reference/fluffos-2.9-ds2.08/comm.c:1693-1704` (`new_user()`: sends `IAC DO TTYPE` before `IAC DO NAWS` before `IAC DO MXP`, unprompted, at connection setup -- this driver previously sent only the NAWS byte sequence), `:808-838` (`TS_WILL`: `TELOPT_TTYPE` answers with the `telnet_term_query[]` probe, `IAC SB TTYPE SEND IAC SE` -- a real, previously-unfixed bug in this driver, WILL TTYPE fell through to the default branch and was wrongly refused with `IAC DONT TTYPE`), `:1078-1083` (`TS_SB`'s own `TELOPT_TTYPE` case: a `TELQUAL_IS(0)` response's payload is handed to the mudlib via `apply(APPLY_TERMINAL_TYPE, ip->ob, 1, ORIGIN_DRIVER)`, any other TELQUAL byte is ignored), and `:2894-2904` (`f_request_term_type()`/`f_start_request_term_type()`, the real `request_term_type()`/`start_request_term_type()` efuns -- resend the SB TTYPE SEND probe / the bare DO TTYPE respectively). `applies.h:30` confirms the real apply name, `terminal_type`. **Real finding that reshaped this row's own scope, confirmed by reading comm.c in full rather than assuming the row title's own bundling**: real FluffOS's entire driver-level MTTS mechanism is exactly these three primitives (`terminal_type()` apply, `request_term_type()`, `start_request_term_type()`) -- there is no round-counting state, no previous-answer comparison, and no bitmask table anywhere in the real driver; the actual Mud Terminal Type Standard's multi-round convention (call `request_term_type()` again, compare the new `terminal_type()` value to the last one, stop once it repeats or a third round yields `"MTTS <bitmask>"`) is genuinely mudlib-side in real FluffOS. `src/proto/instruct.md`'s own `MttsHandler` design (an `query_client_flags()` efun doing the bitmask parsing inside the driver) does not match this -- noted here rather than built, the same "real source first" discipline row 3.2's `call_other` capability-grant finding already established. **Built:** `Connection::requestTerminalType()`/`startRequestTerminalType()` (`Connection.cpp`, mirroring `requestWindowSize()`'s own existing shape exactly), `handleNegotiation()`'s `WILL TTYPE` branch (fixing the DONT-refusal bug above), `handleSubnegotiation()`'s new `TELOPT_TTYPE` case (`terminalType_`/`terminalTypeUpdated_`, the same one-shot-flag shape `terminalWidth_`/`windowSizeUpdated_` already use), `Server::onNewConnection()`'s corrected send order (DO TTYPE before DO NAWS, matching real `new_user()`; DO MXP still not sent, MXP stays out of this slice's scope), `Server::handleConnection()`'s new `terminal_type()` apply-firing block (mirroring the existing `window_size()` block exactly), and three efuns in `EfunTable.cpp`: `request_term_type()`, `start_request_term_type()` (both real), and `query_terminal_type(object)` (a driver-added pull-based convenience over the same real push-based data, explicitly not a real FluffOS efun, same precedent as `query_screen_width()`/`query_screen_height()`). **6 new regression tests (839 total, up from 833):** covering the WILL-TTYPE-answered-not-refused fix, the SB TTYPE IS parse and its one-shot flag, the TELQUAL-byte-other-than-IS no-op case, both new efuns' real byte sequences and their silent no-interactive-command-giver no-op, and `query_terminal_type()`'s value read plus its not-interactive throw. **Still open in this row:** MXP (real source exists, `comm.c`'s own `TELOPT_MXP`/`APPLY_MXP_ENABLE`/`APPLY_MXP_TAG`/`f_has_mxp()`, not yet ported), GMCP/MSDP/MSSP (zero real source anywhere in the vendored FluffOS 2.9 tree -- confirmed by grep, these are later FluffOS additions or third-party protocol extensions never present in `ds2.08`; any implementation of them would be external-spec work, not a port, same honesty precedent as row 3.2's `call_other` finding), and the MTTS bitmask-decoding convenience `src/proto/instruct.md` originally wanted, deliberately not built per the finding above.) |

| 3.8 | Boot a real third-party FluffOS mudlib against this driver (dead-souls.net's TMI2, LPUniversity, or LIL) | `tests` | [x] (TMI-2, real, feature-complete boot, real character creation, and real gameplay confirmed 2026-09-02: login, look, inventory, and movement between real rooms all working over a real TCP connection. `temp/tmi2_fluffos_v3.zip` was already vendored (extracted this session to `temp/tmi2_fluffos_v3_extracted/`, gitignored under `temp/`, see CLAUDE.md); `etc/driver_tmi2.cfg` built from that mudlib's own real `bin/config.tmi2`, same derivation discipline as `etc/driver_aethermud.cfg`. **11 real, narrow driver bugs found and fixed** this pass, the same rigor as row 3.9's AetherMUD pass, each with a real citation, a real fix, and a real regression test (844 tests total, up from 839, full suite green throughout, zero regressions at every step): (1) `sscanf()` output arguments restricted to a bare variable name, real grammar.y's own "lvalue_list" allows any indexed lvalue (`adm/obj/master/access.c`'s own `sscanf(lines[i], "(%s)%s", path, lines[i])`) -- `Parser`/`CodeGen` now support an indexed output target via a hidden temp local plus a deferred `IndexAssign`, `OpCode::Sscanf` itself unchanged; (2) `|=`/`&=`/`^=` compound bitwise assignment entirely unlexed (`access[path][name] |= WRITE;`, 30/6/2 real call sites in this one corpus) -- added to `Lexer`'s two-char operator table and both `Parser` compound-op maps and all three `CodeGen` combine-op switches, reusing the existing `OpCode::BitOr/BitAnd/BitXor`; (3) hex integer literals (`0x0010`) not lexed past the leading `0` (`adm/simul_efun/vt100.c`) -- `Lexer::lexNumber()` gained a hex branch, `Parser`'s `IntLiteral` construction reads it with `std::stoll(..., 16)`, decimal literals fully unaffected; (4) a real off-by-one in single-character string indexing, real `interpret.c`'s own `F_INDEX` throws only when `i > SVALUE_STRLEN(sp)` (strictly greater, reading the implicit NUL terminator as 0 at `i == length`), this driver used `>=` -- broke the ubiquitous real LPC idiom `if (lines[i][0] == '#' || lines[i] == "") continue;` for every empty exploded line (`adm/obj/master/groups.c`/`access.c` both); (5) no trailing comma allowed in a call's argument list, real grammar.y's own `expr_list2 ','` production allows one -- `adm/daemons/newuserd.c`'s own `body->set("PATH", AUTO_WIZHOOD);` where `AUTO_WIZHOOD` is a real valueless flag `#define`, cpp's own correct expansion leaves a bare trailing comma; (6) switch `case A..B:` range labels, previously a deliberate `NotImplementedError` since nothing in the bundled mudlib used the form -- `std/user.c`'s own real level-tier ladder (`case 2..3:` etc) does; parsed and compiled via the same short-circuit `(subject>=low)&&(subject<=high)` shape `emitLogicalExpr()` already uses, reusing `OpCode::Gte/Lte/Not/JumpIfFalse/Dup/Pop`, no VM opcode changes; (7) unary `~` (bitwise NOT) entirely unlexed, real grammar.y's own `'~' expr0` at the same precedence as `!` -- `std/user/bitflags.c`'s own `bitflags &= ~(n);` -- new `UnaryOp::BitNot`/`OpCode::BitNot` added lexer through VM, matching real `interpret.c`'s `F_COMPL` exactly including its "Bad argument to ~" message; (8) an absolute quoted `#include "/..."` nested inside a file reached via another absolute include was never resolved -- real cpp has no concept of a mudlib root, and this driver's own single-pass `rewriteAbsoluteIncludes()` only ever rewrote the *outermost* file's own text, never a transitively-`#include`d file's -- `std/object/sec_ob.c` -> `prop.c` -> `prop_logic.c`, confirmed real cpp's own `--sysroot` does not redirect quote-form includes either; fixed by making the rewrite recursive, splicing the real target file's own content directly (the identical operation real cpp already performs for a resolvable include, applied here to the one case it cannot resolve itself), with a real-cycle guard; (9)/(10)/(11) three separate call sites (`ObjectManager::normalizeFilename()` for `inherit`/`clone_object`/`load_object`, `deep_inherit_list()`/`shallow_inherit_list()`/`inherit_list()`'s own output format, and `VM::resolveMudlibPath()` for every file efun) all assumed a leading `/` was already present on every real LPC pathname, silently mis-concatenating a relative-form one (`std/object/sec_ob.c`'s own `inherit "std/object/ob_logic";`; `adm/simul_efun/overrides.c`'s own real security-gated `exec()` override doing `member_array("/std/body.c", deep_inherit_list(to_obj))`, confirmed against real `array.c`'s own `add_slash(pr->filename)` that a leading slash is real, correct FluffOS behavior, not a prior corpus's own possibly-buggy convention; `adm/daemons/ga_server.c`'s own `#define GLOBAL_ALIASES "adm/etc/global_aliases"` fed to `read_file()`) -- all three now ensure exactly one leading `/` rather than assuming or stripping one; item (9) alone was the root cause of "Error connecting to your body" on every character creation (the real `exec()` security gate could never find a match, always denying the body-object handoff), and item (11) alone was the root cause of `look`/`inventory` failing after login (`ga_server`'s own `create()` never actually read its real alias file, cascading into "call_other() couldn't find object" through `do_xverb()`). **Real, well-evidenced issues found and NOT fixed, correctly out of scope:** `include/login.h`/`include/tsh.h`/`adm/simul_efun/overrides.c` all use `#ifdef 0` as a "commented out" idiom -- invalid per the C standard (`#ifdef` requires an identifier, `0` is a pp-number), rejected by any strict modern cpp, real 1990s-era content meeting a modern system cpp, not a driver defect (real modern FluffOS booting this same file against this same system cpp would hit the identical error); `adm/simul_efun/iwrap.c`'s own `str[0..D_IN]` is a real, separate C pp-number tokenization gotcha (`0..D_IN` greedily lexes as one opaque pp-number token by the real C standard's own pp-number grammar, never macro-expanded), also a real cpp behavior, not this driver's; `/cmds/std/_score::cmd_score()`'s own sprintf usage hits the `%=` column-mode gap row 3.9 already found and scoped out, not re-litigated here; the real Intermud3 network daemon (`adm/daemons/network/I3`) cannot reach a real external gateway from this sandboxed test environment, a genuine environmental constraint, not a driver bug, cascading into `adm/daemons/channels.c`'s own I3-channel-list registration. Three of these four (the two `#ifdef 0` files and the I3 line) were given a minimal, clearly-labeled, non-tracked local patch purely to continue live verification past them (`temp/` is gitignored, never committed) -- not a driver change, not a claim that real FluffOS tolerates them. **This closes the real driver-level backlog this row's boot attempt surfaced.** The other two named candidates (LPUniversity, LIL) remain open if a further third-party-mudlib boot is wanted. **2026-09-03 deeper pass, combat/communication/equip (untested by the above): 4 further real driver bugs found and fixed, same rigor -- object-variable redeclaration over an inherited name wrongly hard-erroring instead of shadowing (`std/monster.c`'s own `alias`, blocking every monster from compiling at all); `heart_beat()` never setting up a command giver, so `this_player()` inside any heartbeat body (real `continue_attack()`) read a bad value and aborted every combat round; `restore_object()`'s own "unsavable slot" marker restoring as this driver's internal undefined value instead of real int 0, breaking a real `== 0` check on a stale combat attacker; `living()`'s own default-argument handling wrongly substituting `this_object()` for an explicit non-object argument. Plus one further real, well-evidenced, and deliberately unfixed finding: `file_name()`'s already-documented "no per-clone id" gap breaks TMI-2's own real `hiddenp()`/`visible()` simul_efuns for any cloned player body, silently breaking cross-player `tell`/`who` for two simultaneously connected wizard-status characters -- a real, cross-cutting object-identity change, named and scoped rather than half-built. 849 tests total (up from 844). Full details, citations, and live-verification account in `STATUS.md`'s own 2026-09-03 entry.) |

`evalCost_` is a plain counter unconditionally reset at the top of every `dispatchLine()` call regardless of whether the previous one threw, needing no separate handling either. 3 new regression tests (767 total, up from 764): one command's uncaught error does not close the connection; **One real bug found and diagnosed, not fixed this session (this driver's own instructions: diagnose with rigor, do not force a fix if the investigation itself is already substantial).** `sprintf: %d argument is not an int`, thrown (caught by the mudlib's own `catch()`, not a driver crash) inside `/cmds/mortal/_score.c`'s own `show_experience()`, reached automatically at the end of character creation and again on an explicit `score` command. Investigated whether this is a real driver strictness gap before assuming so: read this driver's own `%d` argument-type check directly (`EfunTable.cpp`'s sprintf implementation) against real FluffOS's own, `temp/reference/fluffos-2.9-ds2.08/sprintf.c:1180-1198` -- **confirmed byte-for-byte faithful**, real FluffOS's own `sprintf()` throws the identical "Incorrect argument type to %d" error for a non-`T_NUMBER` argument to `%d`, it does not silently coerce a float or anything else; this driver's own strictness here is correct, not a bug. Also checked whether this driver's own `(type)` cast (a real, already-documented no-op at codegen, `Parser.cpp:803-811`'s own comment, since this driver "has no static type checker to feed that annotation to") might be the real gap, since the mudlib's own code relies on `(int)`-casting call_other results without any further validation -- confirmed real FluffOS's own cast is *also* exactly a no-op at runtime (`grammar.y:2275-2295`: `$$->type = $1;`, a compile-time-only annotation, only ever erroring under a strict `exact_types` compiler flag most mudlibs do not enable), so this driver's cast semantics are faithful here too, not a second bug. Traced the actual non-int value's origin as far as budget allowed without fully closing it: `show_experience()`'s own `query_next_level_xp()` branches on `RIFTS_D->is_rifts_race()` (a real, heavily-used function, `daemon/rifts.c:147`, confirmed not missing) between two paths, `ADVANCE_D->get_exp()` (`secure/daemon/advance.c`, a real `switch`/`case` over int literals) and an OCC's own `xp_table` (`daemon/occ.c`, real int-literal array constants, e.g. `xp_civilian()`) -- every literal value checked along both paths is genuinely int-typed on inspection, so the non-int value's exact origin remains open. **Categorized:** not a proven driver gap (the two most likely driver-side suspects, `sprintf()` strictness and `(type)` cast semantics, are each individually confirmed faithful to real FluffOS by direct source citation); most likely either a real latent bug in this specific third-party mudlib's own experience-table code (which would reproduce identically on real FluffOS, untested against an actual FluffOS binary to fully confirm) or a subtler, not-yet-located driver-side type-preservation gap somewhere in the mapping-value-to-array-cast-to-index chain this session's own time budget did not fully trace -- worth a dedicated follow-up session specifically to finish that trace, not a same-session fix given the uncertainty. No other errors of any kind surfaced across the rest of this session's own live testing (character creation, inventory, help, movement, quit).

**2026-08-25: the trace above finished, real driver-side bug found and fixed (see `STATUS.md`'s own dated entry for the full narrative).** Instrumented `query_next_level_xp()` directly with `typeof()` tracing (a temporary, since-reverted debug build of `cmds/mortal/_score.c`, confirmed byte-identical to the original afterward via `diff`) and drove a real fresh character through this driver's own `Scheduler`/`VM` end to end -- every single value along both of the prior session's own two suspect paths (`ADVANCE_D->get_exp()`, an OCC's own `xp_table`) came back genuinely `int`, closing off `query_next_level_xp()`'s own return value as the culprit entirely, a real result the prior session's static-only read could not reach. The actual non-int value is `show_experience()`'s *other* local, `exp = (int)who->query_exp()` -- `std/living.c`'s own `query_exp() { return player_data["general"]["experience"]; }`, a **missing, non-column mapping key** on a freshly created character whose experience has never been written. Confirmed against real `mapping.c`'s own `find_in_mapping()`: a genuine miss returns `&const0u`, and real `main.c`'s own `const0u.subtype = T_UNDEFINED;` confirms `T_UNDEFINED` (`lpc.h`: `#define T_UNDEFINED 0x4`) is a *subtype* flag on an ordinary `T_NUMBER` svalue, not a distinct top-level type -- so a missing-key value is genuine `T_NUMBER` at the real C level and passes real `sprintf()`'s own `%d`/`%o`/`%x`/`%c` type check (`sprintf.c:1180`: `carg->type != T_NUMBER`) fine, printing as plain `0`.

**Real, confirmed driver-side gap, fixed:** this driver's own `OpCode::Index` (`VM.cpp`) already correctly returns its own `monostate` (this driver's T_UNDEFINED-equivalent, see `VM.cpp`'s own `asArithmeticOperand()`/`formatNumberForConcat()` comments) for a missing, non-column `map[key]` -- that part was already right, and already participates in arithmetic and string-concatenation exactly like a real 0, per those two functions' own existing, pre-this-row comments. The gap was narrower and specific to one efun: `sprintf()`'s own `%d`/`%o`/`%x`/`%c` argument-type checks (`EfunTable.cpp`) rejected `monostate` outright, unlike every other numeric context in this driver, and unlike real FluffOS's own T_UNDEFINED-is-T_NUMBER unification. Fixed with one new helper, `sprintfNumericArg()` (a local lambda inside the same `registerCoreEfuns`-style block `sprintfImpl` already lives in, captured by it), used at all four affected call sites -- accepts `int64_t` unchanged and treats `monostate` as `0`, matching `asArithmeticOperand()`'s own established exemption exactly. `%s`/`%O` untouched (`%O` already accepted every kind unconditionally; `%s` correctly still requires a real string). The `'*'`/`'.*'` dynamic *field-width* argument checks were deliberately left as strict `int64_t`-only -- zero real evidence anywhere in this mudlib (or any other vendored corpus) of a missing-mapping-key value ever being used as a field width, unlike the *value* argument this row's own real trace found, matching this project's own bounded-to-real-evidence discipline (row 2.12's own precedent, cited there too).

5 new regression tests (756 total, up from 751): `%d` still throws on a genuinely non-numeric argument (a string, mirroring the pre-existing `%c` symmetric test); `%d` on a missing mapping key prints `0`; `%o`/`%x` on a missing mapping key both print `0`; `%c` on a missing mapping key emits a real NUL byte (matching plain `int 0` cast to `char`); and the exact real-world shape this trace found -- a nested mapping lookup where the outer key exists (a real, populated sub-mapping) but the inner key does not, cast through `(int)` (a no-op, unaffected) into a multi-argument `%d` format string matching `show_experience()`'s own real one -- reproducing `Level: 1    XP: 0 / 2000 (next level)` exactly, the corrected real output. The original 751 re-run unchanged (same names, same count) to confirm the fix is additive.

Live-verified against the real running driver (a new `etc/driver_aethermud.cfg` in this main checkout, values taken directly from the mudlib's own bundled `secure/cfg/mudos.cfg`, kept in the repo as a real, reusable boot config rather than a throwaway diagnostic) and the real AetherMUD mudlib specifically, before and after the fix: before, the score panel silently stopped mid-render at the `EXPERIENCE` header row (the driver's own log showing the real `sprintf: %d argument is not an int` failure via `_score::cmd_score()` -> `_score::show_experience()`, both for the automatic end-of-chargen call, caught by `finish_creation()`'s own `catch()`, and for an explicit `score` command, uncaught, surfacing as `[net] ... input handling failed`); after, a full, correct score panel renders every time, `Level: 1    XP: 0 / 2000 (next level)` included, with zero errors of any kind in the driver's own log across several fresh characters.

This driver's own arrow-call dispatch instead threw "call_other: first argument must be an object or a string path" for an array target, surfacing repeatedly in the driver's own log every time a room with inventory reset. **Categorized: real driver-side language-feature gap** (array-form `call_other()`/`->` not supported), confirmed against real `func_spec.c` directly. **2026-08-25 (a further session, same day): the array-form call_other gap above fixed, real semantics confirmed against the actual interpreter-level implementation, not just the func_spec.c signature.** Read real `efuns_main.c`'s own `f__call_other()` directly: `arg[0].type == T_ARRAY` dispatches to `call_all_other(arg[0].u.arr, funcname, num_arg - 2)` (`interpret.c`), whose full real body was read line by line before writing any code:

```c
array_t *call_all_other (array_t * v, const char * func, int numargs) {
  ...
  sp->u.arr = ret = allocate_array(size = v->size);
  for (vptr = v->item, rptr = ret->item; size--; vptr++, rptr++) {
    if (vptr->type == T_OBJECT) ob = vptr->u.ob;
    else if (vptr->type == T_STRING) {
      ob = find_object(vptr->u.string);
      if (!ob || !object_visible(ob)) continue;
    } else continue;
    if (ob->flags & O_DESTRUCTED) continue;
    i = numargs;
    while (i--) push_svalue(tmp - i);
    call_origin = ORIGIN_CALL_OTHER;
    if (apply_low(func, ob, numargs)) *rptr = *sp--;
  }
  ...
}
```

Exact real semantics this confirms, answering every question this row's own instructions posed directly from source, not inferred: (1) **the result is a real array, the same size as the input, index-aligned in the same order** (`allocate_array(size = v->size)`, `vptr`/`rptr` incrementing together in lockstep) -- not a "drop the misses" shape. (2) **Every unfilled slot defaults to real `allocate_array()`'s own plain `const0`** (`array.c`: `while (n--) p->item[n] = const0;`), an ordinary, subtype-less int 0 -- confirmed distinct from the T_UNDEFINED-tagged `const0u` this row's own earlier sprintf fix had to special-case (`main.c`: only `const0u.subtype = T_UNDEFINED;`, `const0` gets no such tag). (3) **A destructed array element is silently skipped, not an error** (`if (ob->flags & O_DESTRUCTED) continue;`), its own slot simply staying at the default 0. (4) **A missing function on one element is likewise silently skipped, not an error** (`if (apply_low(...))` false just means the `*rptr = *sp--;` line never runs for that element) -- confirmed this is the exact same "one bad element never aborts the whole call" rule the destructed case uses, not a separate path. (5) A string element resolves via `find_object()` the same way the scalar-target form already does, skipping (not erroring) on a miss. (6) The same args are pushed for every element (`while (i--) push_svalue(tmp - i);` reads from the one saved `tmp` stack position every iteration, not a per-element slice).

**Real corpus cross-check, confirming this is genuinely needed, not a speculative generalization:** the `array_var->method(args)` idiom is real and widespread, not narrow to AetherMUD -- confirmed live across every vendored corpus this project tracks: `dead-souls` (`std/room.c`'s own `all_inventory()->reset(count)`, `npc.c`'s own `all_inventory()->eventDestruct()`, and others), `lima` (`daemons/loot_d.c`'s own four chained `all_inventory(adversary)->...` calls), `es2_mudlib`, `nightmare3`, this project's own bundled `mudlib/` corpus (`temp/mudlib`), and AetherMUD's own `reinitiate()` itself, the real call site that surfaced this gap. One real usage confirms the *result-array* semantics specifically matter, not just the dispatch: `lima/lib/secure/simul_efun/userfuncs.c`'s own `users()->query_body() - ({0})` explicitly filters the literal-0 non-hit entries back out of the result -- live proof real mudlib code relies on exactly the "unfilled slot is int 0" fallback this row's own real-source reading found, not some other sentinel. No real corpus evidence of the *combined* array-of-targets-plus-array-of-(funcname,boundargs) form (real FluffOS's own separate, dual-array shape) anywhere in any vendored corpus, and this driver's own scalar-target `call_other` never supported an array-form function name/bound-args either -- that combination stayed out of scope, not a gap in this fix, matching this project's own bounded-to-real-evidence discipline (row 2.12's own precedent).

**Built:** the array-form branch added to `call_other`'s own registered efun body (`EfunTable.cpp`), checked before the existing scalar (`object`/`string`) branches. Reuses `VM::callFunction()` directly as the per-element apply mechanism -- it already implements the destructed-target and missing-function "return a default `Value{}`, never throw" behavior real `call_all_other()` needs for free (confirmed directly, not assumed, against that method's own header comment), so the new code only has to: resolve each element (`shared_ptr<LpcObject>` used directly, a string resolved via `vm.findObject()`, anything else left null), call through `VM::callFunction()` when a target was resolved, and collapse the result into the output array -- converting this driver's own `monostate` (whether from a skip or from a genuinely void function falling through, both observably identical to a real int 0 on real FluffOS too, since a void function's own real bytecode still pushes a real 0 before returning) into a real `int64_t{0}`, matching real `allocate_array()`'s own plain `const0` default exactly.

5 new regression tests (761 total, up from 756): array-form call_other calling every element and returning results in the right order; a destructed element silently skipped, its own slot left at 0, the rest of the array still called; a missing function on one element likewise skipped, the rest still called; a string element resolved via `find_object()` to the real blueprint object; and the exact real reinitiate() shape itself (`obs->move(dest)`, return value discarded, only each element's own real environment change checked afterward) confirming the side effect actually happens for every element, not just the first. The original 756 re-run unchanged.

Live-verified against the real running driver and the real AetherMUD mudlib specifically, before and after: before, walking a fresh character through the same Chi-Town rooms row 3.9's own broader pass already exercised reliably reproduced `[call_out] .../reinitiate(): ... call_other: first argument must be an object or a string path` in the driver's own log every time; after, zero such errors across the same walk. **Combat exercised for real, `kill thug`, live-verified against the running driver:** hit/miss/parry/dodge resolution all fired with real variety across many rounds ("Burb thug missed you.", "You parry Burb thug's attack!", "Burb thug dodges your inept attack.", "Your attack goes wide, hitting nothing but air."); armor absorption and destruction fired for real ("Burb thug's attack glances off your armor." repeatedly, then "Your leather jacket is destroyed!" once its own SDC pool was exhausted, matching `daemon/rifts_combat.c`'s own real SDC-armor-then-character-SDC-then-HP damage priority -- the player's own `rifts_hp` correctly never moved across the whole fight because the thug's hits were still being absorbed by armor/SDC, not HP, real Palladium behavior, not a bug). Death/incapacitation: the code paths exist and read correctly on inspection (`cur_hp <= -10` dies, `cur_hp <= 0` falls unconscious, both in `daemon/rifts_combat.c`), but were not actually reached live within this session's own budget (the thug's SDC pool alone absorbed roughly a minute of real combat without exhausting) -- not ruled in or out, reported honestly rather than claimed.

**One real, well-evidenced bug found in the mudlib's own combat daemon, documented in full, not fixed (out of this driver project's scope -- this project runs real mudlibs correctly, it does not patch their own content, regardless of size).** `daemon/rifts_combat.c`'s own `apply_rifts_damage()` carries an explicit doc comment describing its own intended return contract: `-1: messages already sent, caller skips send_messages`, `0: bounced/parried, no damage`, `>0: damage dealt, caller skips send_messages`. Read the entire function's own body, every `return` statement (14 total, confirmed by direct grep): every single one is either `0` (only the earliest degenerate cases -- invalid args, an impervious-element hit) or `-1` -- **no code path anywhere in the function ever returns the actual damage dealt**, contradicting its own documented `>0` case outright. Confirmed live, not just from source: a temporary debug build of `std/living/combat.c` (reverted afterward, confirmed byte-identical via `diff`) traced a real attack computing a genuine 9-point raw damage value, passed into `apply_rifts_damage()`, which still returned exactly `-1`. Real consequence, traced through the caller (`std/living/combat.c`'s own attack loop): `if(x > 0) { award_full_xp_and_skill_points... }` never fires for any Rifts-race combatant (nearly every real fight in this content, since "human" and most playable races are Rifts-flagged) -- **no combat XP or skill points are ever awarded through this path for a Rifts-race fight**, a real, significant progression-breaking bug in the mudlib's own content. Real, direct-armor-hit damage still applies correctly regardless (the function mutates `SDC`/`rifts_hp`/`MDC` via `set_stats()` calls directly, independent of its own broken return value), so combat is not fully non-functional, only its XP/skill-reward feedback loop is broken. Also noted, found incidentally while reverting the debug build, an unrelated real discrepancy worth naming for whoever picks this up: `std/living/combat.c`'s own `continue_attack()` (the heart_beat-driven death check) reads `player_data["general"]["hp"]` for its own death condition, a completely different stat key from `rifts_hp` (the one `apply_rifts_damage()` and `_score.c`'s own display both actually use) -- not investigated further this session, named rather than silently found and dropped.

**Two real driver-side gaps found and scoped, deliberately not fixed this session (both real, but not "small, well-evidenced" one-line fixes the way the sprintf/call_other fixes were -- scoped and deferred per this row's own explicit instruction not to rush a big fix):**

1. **sprintf's `%=` (column mode) is genuinely needed, real corpus evidence now confirmed.** `cmds/mortal/_emote.c`'s own `cmd_emote()` uses it; hitting it during ordinary play throws `sprintf: unsupported format specifier '%='`. This was already a known, deliberately scoped-out limitation (this driver's own sprintf implementation comment already lists `"="` (column mode) among several real modifiers left unimplemented, alongside `"#"`/`"@"`/`"'X'"`/`" "`/`"+"`), not a newly-discovered blind spot -- what changed this session is real, direct corpus evidence that a genuine, reachable AetherMUD command needs it, upgrading it from "not yet needed" to "confirmed real need," worth prioritizing whenever sprintf's remaining modifier set gets picked up.
2. **A real driver-side error-isolation gap, a genuine divergence from real semantics.** Hitting the `%=` error above did not just fail that one `emote` command -- it closed the entire connection (`Server.cpp`'s own `handleConnection()`: `catch (const std::exception& e) { ... markClosed() }` around the whole per-line dispatch, a deliberate design choice per that code's own comment, "a runtime error handling one player's input line must close only that player's connection, not crash the driver out from under everyone else"). Checked against real FluffOS's own architecture before concluding this is a real gap, not just a style difference: real `process_input()`/`process_user_command()` (`comm.c`) has no per-line try/catch of its own at all -- real error recovery in FluffOS is a C-level `SETJMP`/`error_recovery_context` wrapped once around each entire top-level task (a player command, a heart_beat, a call_out fire) by `backend.c`'s own dispatch loop, catching an uncaught LPC error, logging it, and returning control to the *same still-open* interactive session for its next command -- it does **not** disconnect the player over one bad command. This driver's own connection-level catch is coarser than that: a real, single divergence, confirmed by architecture comparison, not by exhaustively replicating FluffOS's own SETJMP task model here. A real fix belongs to a dedicated session of its own (a genuine architectural change to `Server::dispatchLine()`'s own error-handling shape, not a bounded one-line fix), not attempted here.

Test-account/postal state created during this session's own live verification (6 accounts, matching `postal/` files, plus incidental `daemon/save/*.o` touched by real play) removed afterward, matching this row's own established cleanup precedent. Driver source code was not modified this session (investigation and reporting only); 761 tests unchanged, confirmed by re-running `build/test/amlp_tests` directly, not assumed.

## Build and test

```bash
cmake -B build -S .
cmake --build build
ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure
```

Current baseline: **677 tests passing** (as of 2026-08-19; this figure
lags behind each row's own per-slice count above between updates: treat
STATUS.md's own most recent entry as authoritative if the two ever
disagree). Every new slice must pass the full suite before merging.

---

## Sequencing principle

- **Never break the current test baseline** (see above). All work is incremental slices.
- **Phase 0 before Phase 1.** A buggy foundation makes dialect work meaningless.
- **Phase 1 before Phase 2.** Dialect abstraction unlocks concurrent dialect work.
- **Read the instruct.md in the target directory first.** Each one lists exact
  files to read, exact reference sources, and the precise scope of its tasks.
- **One slice = one PR.** Keep changes small, reviewable, and revertable.
